# Port-Ledger

## THE COMPREHENSIVE AUDIT, 2026-08-31: two sweeps for the session's own bug class

Six times in one session a call reached a seam that was not there - a
method that did not exist, a field nobody read, an option the callee
never destructured (`openUseMagicItem`, `openTransport`,
`audio.setLoop`, `dismountOnTransition`, `t.reposition`,
`shadowText({scale})`). Three were inherited and three were written the
same day. So the audit stopped reading features and swept for the
SHAPE:

1. **Options a callee does not accept.** Every exported function with a
   destructured options bag, against every call site passing an object
   literal. 49 suspects, all inspected: false positives - the pattern
   catches nested literals in later arguments (`loftPiece({ y })`,
   `drawHudLarge`'s `vitalsBars: { rig }`). No finding, and the sweep is
   too noisy to keep as a test.
2. **Optional-chained calls with no implementation anywhere.** Every
   `x?.()` whose method name is defined in no module. 19 hits. Eighteen
   are optional BY DESIGN - a per-candidate override with a documented
   fallback (`stealthOf`), a browser API (`webkitGetAsEntry`,
   `setPointerCapture`), a Worker's `terminate`, or an arm that defers
   LOUDLY (the quest clock's `travelSeconds` stores
   `travelTimePending = true` and zero seconds rather than a wrong
   number - the anti-lie law working).
   **One was real:** no host supplied `specialDungeonName` or
   `dungeonRegionName`, so "Where am I?" asked underground formatted
   with two empty strings. Fixed, with GetSpecialDungeonName ported.

The lesson for the next reader: sweep 2 is worth re-running after any
slice that adds a deps bag, and its signal is high because the
false-positive classes are small and nameable. Sweep 1 is not worth
keeping - a real signature check needs a parser, not a regex.

## BOOT FAILURE, 2026-08-31: `const X = { ...X }` (UI1)

The live site failed to boot with "can't access lexical declaration
'hi' before initialization". `hi` was the minified `useHooks`, and the
source read `const useHooks = { ...useHooks };` - a scripted edit in
UI1 had replaced the wrong occurrence of a block, so the declaration
ate itself. Three things let it through and each is worth knowing:

1. **eslint does not see it.** `no-use-before-define` does not look
   inside an initialiser's own spread.
2. **The build does not see it.** It minifies to `hi={...hi}` and
   ships.
3. **No test executes the module body.** `world.js`, `worldModes.js`
   and the other big hosts cannot be imported under bare node - Vite's
   `import.meta.glob` throws - so every pin on them is a TEXT pin, and
   the two pins covering this line asserted that `...useHooks`
   APPEARED. It did. Inside itself.

`test/tdz_selfreference.test.js` is the guard, and the deeper lesson is
about the pins: a text pin that asserts a fragment EXISTS cannot tell
where it exists. Where a pin cannot execute the code, it should assert
the SHAPE around the fragment (what precedes and follows it), not the
fragment alone.

**The blind spot, measured** (`test/moduleload_smoke.test.js`, the same
day): 461 of the port's 470 modules import cleanly under bare node, so
"nothing executes the module bodies" was true of far less of the tree
than it felt. The smoke now runs all 461 and asserts the remaining NINE
exactly - three on Vite's `import.meta.glob` (`main.js`,
`scenes/questData.js`, `scenes/world.js`) and six browser tools on
`document`/`location`. The list is checked both ways, so an exclusion
can never quietly cover a real break. Shrinking those three - lifting
the glob call behind a seam - would put the port's most-edited file
under a body-executing test, and is worth doing the next time
`world.js` is opened for structural work.

Single source of truth for everything that is not a plain 1:1 line. Three
categories. If a departure or gap is not on this page, it does not exist.
Adding to category A requires Mac approval; B records data reality; C is the
work queue routed to arcs.

## A. Approved departures from DFU
- Slot-0 random-enemy reroll: DFU seeds UnityEngine.Random with DateTime.Now.Ticks (their TODO comment); we seed a xorshift32 with the dungeon LocationId so layouts are deterministic and testable.

ROTOR_HUB [3.96, 6.01, -5.5] is the prefab's own offset in model 41600's space, not a guess. WM2b also found a defect in WM1: rotorMatrix CONJUGATES about the hub, right for a sail already in place and wrong for the vendored origin-centred rotor, which it would ORBIT rather than spin - mountRotor added, both pinned apart. WM2d (2026-08-29) FOUND WHY NOTHING APPEARED: classic Daggerfall stands NO windmill at all. WM2a had read model 41600 out of Kamer's WorldData block overrides and taken it for Daggerfall's, but a WorldData override REPLACES a block and his are his own - FARMAA01's declares one subrecord and carries two, FARMAA00's puts the mill in subrecord 7 of a seven-record block. So WM2b matched a model id that could never match and every pin passed, because every pin tested the ROTOR and none tested that a MILL EXISTED. The port places its own now: his tower (`vendor/windmills-kamer/Windmill.dae`, five texture groups bound from the prefab's material list, refused if the map is incomplete) at the six placements he chose (`placements.json` - only the ADDED record from each block; a WorldData block is Daggerfall's layout and stays out), built by `rmbLayout.windmillsFor` so a mill's matrix is the same two lines as every other placed model's. Both hosts draw the tower with a collider and the sail per frame; WINDMILL_MODELS is retired, table and flag together. WM2e (2026-08-29, Mac's screenshot - the mills stand but the sail hangs beside one and the tower stands on a path): ONE root cause, the HANDEDNESS. Collada is right-handed and Unity is left-handed, and every number the mod carries is written in Unity's space - the bake negates X now (normals mirrored, winding reversed), so the hub +3.96 meets a cap centred at 3.94 instead of its mirror at -3.95, and the tower stops swinging eight units across its anchor. ROTOR_SIGN flips with it and that is not a disagreement: a mirror reverses the sense of a rotation about an axis in its plane, so his -13 deg/s and our +13 turn the sails the same way. Every pin passed through the bug because all eleven asked about the ROTOR ALONE and none asked whether it MEETS THE MILL; they do now. WM2e also carries his CLIMATE AND SEASON SKINS verbatim (`variants.json` - seventeen variant prefabs, only the walls and roof ever differ, and the desert mill never winters, which is his prefabs and ClimateSwaps.cs agreeing), applied by uploading one body mesh per climate rather than through the location's texRemap, which is keyed over the whole scene and would have re-skinned every building sharing 364_2. WM3 (2026-08-29) OVERLAPPED WM2e AND WAS OVERTAKEN BY IT, and the record is kept because the near-miss is the useful part: WM3 was going to run the mill through the location's climate table, which WM2e's own note rules out - and the pins now say WHY from the data. The walls agree exactly (his wall ARCHIVE is ApplyClimate's in every base and both seasons; only the record differs, which the climate law never touches), but HALF the summer roofs part company - he keeps the temperate 369 in mountain and swamp where the law rebases to 169 and 469 - and every winter roof is his one snow roof 103_1, which ApplyClimate produces nowhere. WM3 also corrected the sentence this row used to carry: the ROTOR was never going to swap, TEXTURE.000 record 77 and TEXTURE.067 record 1 classifying into no set at all, which is ClimateSwaps agreeing from the other side with Kamer's 067_1 sail in all seventeen prefabs. What WM3 DID ship is the seam: the eleven-line remap loop had four copies (exterior, world, interiors, dungeons) and now has one home in src/world/texRemap.js, taking the LAW as its argument - no host writes texRemap any more. WM2f (2026-08-29, Mac: "the door is covered and I can't enter it"): the mill comes with a CLASSIC BUILDING. Every one of his subrecords places TWO models - 118 (the structure with the real door) and 41600 (the mill) - and WM2d read only the mill, because those subrecords declare Num3dObjectRecords 1 while carrying 2, the same hand-edit slip as FARMAA01 declaring one subrecord and carrying two. The building rides the ordinary model path (mesh, climate swap, collider, doors) tagged enhancedOnly so the 1:1 lane never sees it, and carries NO recordIndex: its interior lives in a subrecord he ADDED, which the port's block does not have, so worldModes' existing undefined-recordIndex guard keeps the door from throwing. THE MILL IS STILL NOT ENTERABLE AND THAT IS FAITHFUL - his exterior has NumDoorRecords 0 in all six blocks, so it is not enterable in his mod either; making it so would need his added subrecord's 44-model INTERIOR, which is Daggerfall block data and Mac's call. WM2g (2026-08-29, Mac asked twice and then said "Put it in"): THE MILL HAS AN INSIDE. vendor/windmills-kamer/interior.json is the interior of the subrecord he adds to each farm block, identical in all six; scripts/bakeWindmillInterior.mjs converts it into blocksFile's own parsed shape and rmbLayout.attachWindmillRecord appends it as the subrecord the block is missing, so the building's door resolves through the ordinary machinery and nothing downstream knows where it came from. The attach is IDEMPOTENT (BlocksFile caches parsed blocks; a second append would move the recordIndex a live door refers to) and its exterior is EMPTY (or every mill would stand twice). Header counts are rebuilt from the arrays: he declares 44 models, 12 flats and 10 doors over arrays of 16, 11 and 0. WM2g first recorded this as BLOCK DATA and an approved departure; WM2h corrects it, on Mac's word - "why not his own work? that was the whole point". IT IS HIS ROOM: the subrecord is not in the block Daggerfall ships (he ADDED it), its interior matches no other subrecord's in the same block, the room is ~12.4 x 12.2 world units against his own 16 x 27 x 18 mill body so it was built to fit it, and its centrepiece is model 41601 - the machinery he modelled AND animated. Same kind of artifact as placements.json: model ids and coordinates the author chose, the ids Daggerfall's exactly as in every placement list this port ships. Naming a model is not shipping one; no Daggerfall art or bytes are in the file, so this is NOT a departure. NOBODY HAS SEEN IT RUN: no GL and no ARENA2 here, so the wiring pins are source sweeps and the sail's placement on the classic tower is the outstanding one-look question. The rotor takes no climate swap yet and there is no sound. What is taken from his CODE is still a reading, credited in the module header - the blade assembly turns about the model's local Z, and it turns at 13 degrees a second. THE 13 IS THE ANCHOR AND THE REST IS OURS: where the mod turns at 13 in a gale and a dead calm alike, our rotor turns at 13 in FAIR WEATHER and takes the rest from the wind - ROTOR_GAIN is DERIVED from `WEATHER_SKY.sunny.wind` rather than written down, so a re-tuned sky row cannot quietly stop meaning "13 in fair weather". The wind is ES1c's eased per-weather row, imported and not restated, which buys the property worth having: the blades and the clouds are driven by the same number, so a storm picks the mill up on the same fourteen-second curve it picks the sky up on. Skin constants (STALL_WIND, FURL_DEG_PER_SEC) are stated as skin and pinned for STRUCTURE - still below the stall, monotone in the wind, never past the furl - not asserted back at themselves. windmills.test.js carries the campaign (15 mutants, 14 killed, 1 proved equivalent) | Mac, 2026-08-29 - the WindMills.rar handed over as the reference, on the roads' own precedent: "instead of taking their mod, I want us to develop our own and better" |

DFU's console UI is not DFU source: `ConsoleUI.cs` and `ConsoleController.cs` are the third-party **Wenzil UnityConsole** addon under `Assets/Game/Addons/UnityConsole/`, and they are a Unity uGUI front end - an InputField, a ScrollRect, a Scrollbar and a Text whose LAYOUT lives in a prefab that is not in the reference tree - driven by EventSystem selection state (`EventSystem.current.alreadySelecting`), CanvasGroup raycast blocking, Unity's text-mesh length cap (maxOutputLength 12000) and `inputField.ActivateInputField()`. There is no 1:1 to port; a window drawn in the port's own idiom would be the port's design, not DFU's. What IS DFU-adjacent source and DOES ship whole is everything behind that window: the ConsoleCommandsDatabase, ConsoleCommand, NoSuchCommandException, Console.Log, HelpCommand, ConsoleInputHistory and ConsoleController.ExecuteCommand's submit law, with all eight registered map commands (`map_revealbuildings`/`map_hidebuildings`, `map_revealall`/`map_hideall`/`map_teleportmode`, `map_reveallocations`/`map_hidelocations`/`map_reveallocation`) reachable through `installConsoleProbe()` - the port's own global console door, mounted by every scene host beside its other developer verbs, honouring `Enhancements/LypyL_GameConsole` exactly as ConsoleUI.ToggleConsole does (:51-53). | **THE ENGINE-PRNG RULE: a UnityEngine.Random draw rides an injectable uniform roll; a DFRandom or System.Random draw does not** | AUDIT 21 (doctrine lane, F7) found ELEVEN sites in `src/` citing "the Ledger A engine-PRNG rule" - `nameHelper.js`, `chargen.js` (three), `talkSession.js`, `talkTopics.js`, `classQuestions.js`, `townTalk.js`, `dungeonContext.js` (two) and `shared.js` - and this page had no such row. The two randomness rows that existed are scoped to the dungeon slot-0 reroll and to terrain noise / nature scatter, and under this file's own charter ("if a departure or gap is not on this page, it does not exist") eleven substitutions were unapproved. They were also unreviewable: nobody could tell which had been reasoned about and which assumed. THE RULE, stated once: where DFU draws from `UnityEngine.Random`, the stream is unseeded and not reproducible outside Unity, so the port takes an INJECTABLE uniform roll (defaulting to `Math.random`) and pins it for distribution, never for sequence. Where DFU draws from `DFRandom` or `System.Random`, the draw is DATA - a classic-compatible sequence - and stays byte-exact; `shared.js` had to work that distinction out on its own, which is what this row prevents. New `UnityEngine.Random` sites cite THIS row by name | Approved (rule stated at AUDIT 21; the sites predate it) |
| Engine-internal randomness (terrain noise + nature scatter: `src/world/perlin.js`, `src/world/terrainNature.js`) | Ken Perlin reference improved noise (perlin.js) in place of Unity's Mathf.PerlinNoise, and umRandom seeded by the verbatim terrain key in place of UnityEngine.Random for nature scatter - neither engine PRNG is in DFU source; same role and statistics, different concrete samples; all pins pin our pipeline. The beach-line jitter and NextInt/NextFloat primitives are NOT departures - Unity.Mathematics Random is open MIT source and umRandom.js is a byte-exact 1:1 translation | APPROVED by Mac (option A, post-M9 audit): the substitutes are canonical for this port; bit parity with Unity engine PRNGs is out of scope |
| THE QUEST ITEM MINT PENDS MAGIC.DEF HEADLESS, `src/systems/quest/item.js` (Q2b-ii) | Item.cs's MagicItems and Artifacts arms build from the MAGIC.DEF registry (ItemBuilder.CreateRegularMagicItem / DaggerfallUnityItem.SetArtifact - both ported whole in `src/systems/loot.js`). The quest machine layer runs with NO ARENA2 (the corpus gate's charter), so when no host has registered the file (loot.js setMagicItemTemplates - the dungeon context does at boot) the mint returns a `pendingMagicDef` stand-in instead of throwing: the quest PARSES, the Item resource stands, the link stands, and the 42 magic_item + 31 artifact corpus declarations are counted by a pin. A host that runs quests without registering MAGIC.DEF would hand the player a pending stand-in - the Q4 wiring slice must register the file before scheduling quests. Books ride the same stance thinner: the message half pends the book catalog, and ~~the value half pends the E1 book-file-pricing row~~ is closed - ROAD-A A2 routes both quest-book mints through `systems/books.js` (`quest/item.js:238-239`, `createBook`/`createRandomBook`), so a quest book carries the BOOK FILE's price when a host warmed the registry and the template basePrice, loudly, when none did | Q2b-ii: the corpus gate must run with no ARENA2 anywhere |
| PlaySound's SND RESOLUTION stays host-side, `src/systems/quest/actions.js` (Q2b-i) | PlaySound.cs resolves SoundReader.GetSoundIndex + GetAudioClip AT CREATE inside its try/catch, so in DFU a sound the SND archive cannot serve nulls the action and the line pends. The quest machine layer runs with NO ARENA2 (the corpus gate's charter), so the port's create resolves only the Quests-Sounds table id - an unknown NAME still nulls and pends, verbatim - and the index/clip lookup rides the playSound hook at play time. Observable difference: a corpus line naming a table-known sound whose SND record is unreadable RESOLVES here where DFU would pend it; the hook then plays nothing. No such line exists in the vendored corpus (all 3 resolve on both sides) | Q2b-i: the corpus gate must run with no ARENA2 anywhere |

| VidFile's frame-buffer overrun THROWS explicitly (U22) | C# indexes a Color32[] and an overrun raises IndexOutOfRangeException, which VidFile's catch swallows into the parked-stream quirk above. A JS typed array DROPS an out-of-range write silently, so a faithful transcription would have COMPLETED the block C# aborts - the quirk would have been lost by porting the code correctly. An explicit bounds throw restores DFU's actual behaviour | Mechanism differs so behaviour matches |
| The SPLASH ingest: VIDs are named ONE AT A TIME (U22) | The diet drops all 86MB of .VID and a blanket rule would undo its single biggest saving, so only a video the port actually PLAYS is ingested - ANIM0001 the splash (1.4MB) and, since D1 wired the death sequence, ANIM0012 (the MERGE AUDIT caught this sentence still saying ANIM0001 alone, thirteen lines above the KEEP that names the second one). DFU names four more - ANIM0000/ANIM0011/DAG2 the new-game cinematics at 26.6MB, ANIM0002/ANIM0004 the dream videos - and none of THOSE is wired here yet. Ingesting a file nobody plays costs every user the bytes for nothing; forgetting one that IS played is the F2 silent degradation - so the rule is enforced by AUDIT 18 F2's sweep, which already covered the splash the moment main.js named it | Storage budget (the 2026-08-14 diet) |
| VIDEO PRESENTATION, src/ui/videoPlayer.js (U22) | Three engine-side departures the file cites and that AUDIT 19 found had NO Ledger row - the exact 17m shape (a comment pointing at a row that does not exist). (1) Unity uploads Color32[] into a Texture2D; here the frame rides renderer.uploadTexture under one key, released before each upload so the memoised texture is replaced rather than leaked per frame. (2) DFU inverts rect Y at draw because Unity textures are bottom-up; ours are top-down (the nativePanel/IMG convention), so the frame draws straight. (3) DFU's two-AudioSource flip-flop pool is dropped - a WebAudio buffer source is one-shot and self-owned. That third one was ALSO overstated: the file said the pool 'has no observable effect', and it does - DFU's pool bounds concurrent clips at two, so after a long host stall we schedule more than DFU would | Engine difference (WebAudio/WebGL) |
| The GM VOICE BANK is SYNTHESISED - TWO-OPERATOR FM, src/systems/gmSynth.js (A5/A5b) | The instrument source, which the music C row said needed deciding. DFU renders its own .mid files with a vendored AudioSynthesis synth and a SoundFont from Unity Resources - both DFU's assets, not the user's ARENA2 - so there is neither a synth to port nor an instrument set to read. The archive needs 52 distinct GM programs and 76 of its 131 songs put notes on channel 9, so any sample answer means shipping megabytes of our own or asking the player for an SF2. Oscillators ship nothing and ask for nothing, which is the only option that works the moment the page loads; it is also the worst sounding, so the bank sits BEHIND AN INTERFACE (voiceSpec/percussionSpec are pure descriptions) and an SF2 or sample bank replaces it later without touching the scheduler. A5b then replaced the subtractive bank with 2-OPERATOR FM, which is the synthesis Daggerfall was actually scored for - AdLib/SoundBlaster OPL2/OPL3 - and is why the archive carries an F*/FM* arrangement of nearly every song. There is NO OPL patch bank to read either: it lived in HMI's sound driver, ARENA2 has no .AD/.BNK/.OPL and no driver, and the song headers carry a device/channel map and zeros where patches would be (checked byte by byte before writing it). So the ratios and indices are ours on the same terms as before, but the METHOD is period-correct rather than arbitrary, and it subsumes the old bank - modulation index 0 is a bare carrier. The specs are taste and are NOT pinned as truth - the pins assert structure only  **AUDITED 2026-08-25** after the music was reported as sounding wrong, and it was, in three ways - all of them in the bank rather than the data path. (1) THE DRUM MAP STOPPED AT NOTE 51 while percussionSpec resolves by nearest neighbour, so every key above it played the ride cymbal: measured over the real archive, 25 of the 30 drum notes in use and 14,000+ hits, because Daggerfall's percussion is hand drums and bells rather than a kit. The map runs 35..87 now, and gained a `drop` field - a falling pitch is what makes a drum read as a drum, and holding pitch is what makes a triangle or claves read as itself. (2) ARTICULATION BEATS FAMILY: `program >> 3` put harp and timpani in `strings` and rendered them BOWED, so eight programs the archive plays now override their family. (3) THE SUSTAIN PEDAL was dropped entirely, and it is the only ignored controller that changes what is heard - CC123/CC121 matter to a device that must be told to stop, and this scheduler cannot hang a note because an HMI note-on carries its own duration. The DATA path was cleared with evidence and is unchanged: no tempo meta events in any of the 131 songs, CC105 is a per-track marker (1,286 ones and 1,286 zeros over 1,286 tracks), no RPN sets a bend range, and pan reaches a real panner. |
| The SONG SCHEDULER, src/systems/songPlayer.js (A5) | WebAudio lookahead rather than per-note timers: the loop wakes on a coarse interval and schedules the next window with sample-accurate start times, so a tab that stalls does not stutter. The SHAPE is forced by the data rather than chosen - one tempo per song and no tempo meta anywhere in the archive, so secondsPerTick is constant and tick->time is a multiply, which is why there is no tempo map. Songs LOOP, because they are 4-44s cues and ending in silence is wrong | Engine difference (WebAudio) |
| ~~Two selection arms are NOT portable as written (A5)~~ **STRUCK - SHIPPED at AUDIT 19** | AUDIT 21 (doctrine lane, F2): this row said the Sneaking/MagesGuild arm and the dungeon arm were unported and "BOTH fall back to DFU's own day-seeded 'most other places' branch". Both are ported and live - the unseeded arm at `songManager.js:149-183`, the dungeon seed at `dungeonKey(unknown2, regionIndex)` and threaded from three hosts (`dungeonContext.js` musicSeed, `worldModes.js`, `dungeon.js`) - and section C already records them as shipped. The Ledger's own rule two rows down is RETIRING A ROW STRIKES THE ROW, and this is the second time that rule has been broken by adding a fresh row instead. What IS approved here, and all that is: the unseeded arm has no reproducible UnityEngine.Random stream outside Unity, so its `random` is injectable and pinned for uniformity only | Struck (shipped) |
| The MIDI.BSA SONG READER, src/formats/hmiFile.js | NO DFU SOURCE EXISTS. DFU never reads this archive: DaggerfallSongPlayer.cs loads 133 pre-converted .mid files plus a SoundFont from Unity Resources and renders them with its vendored AudioSynthesis synth; SongFiles.cs is an enum and PlaySong.cs a quest action. So there is no reader to port AND no reusable asset - DFU's .mid/SF2 are DFU's own, not the user's ARENA2. This reader was written against the SHIPPED BYTES of the user's own MIDI.BSA and corpus-gated over all 131 songs / 1286 tracks. The commission said XMI and the bytes refused - no FORM/XDIR/CAT/XMID chunk exists anywhere in the archive; it is HMI Sound Operating System, signature HMI-MIDISONG061595, sharing exactly one trait with XMI (note-on carries a duration) and differing in the delta encoding (ordinary MIDI VLQs, not interval counts). The file is named for the format it reads, not the commission. Sizes proven and meanings NOT are exposed raw and say so; anything unrecognised throws with song and offset rather than decoding to garbage | Established from data (no source law to be 1:1 with) |
| The TITLE SCREEN prefers OUR logo, and falls back to TITL00I0 (U21c/U21d) | Classic's title art is TITL00I0.IMG. DFU does not draw it - TITL00I0 appears nowhere in the DFU source except ImgFile's palettized file list, because DFU replaced classic's title with its OWN branding. Our analogue of that is our own logo, so ui/titleScreen.js draws public/logo.png (the first non-ARENA2 image the port loads - OUR artwork is ours and WOULD ship with the build; game data still never enters the repo. AUDIT 19: public/logo.png is NOT in the repo, so this arm never runs today and the TITL00I0 fallback is what every boot draws). Centred, aspect-preserved, capped at 86% of the canvas, any click or key advances. If the file is absent the title screen does not draw AT ALL and the boot goes straight to the menu - a missing asset costs a splash, never a game. U21d then added the FALLBACK, and it is not a placeholder: when public/logo.png is absent the screen draws CLASSIC'S OWN TITLE, out of the user's ARENA2 at runtime - so the port has a real title screen with nothing to ship and nothing to install, and swaps to ours the moment the file exists. Drawing classic's title where classic drew it is the least surprising thing to do while our art is missing, so this row now covers both arms. TITL00I0 is one of the SIX palettized IMGs and gets its own DFPalette. The two arms draw by DIFFERENT laws, deliberately: classic art takes the native panel at integer scale with the 1-bit cutout and NEAREST, ours takes logoRect with blending and LINEAR - each law is wrong for the other's art. This retires the C row that said showing a title needed an A row first. The art ALSO needed two opt-ins the classic path does not want: alpha BLENDING (every screen quad in the port discards a<0.5 and forces alpha 1, which is exactly right for classic art because classic art IS a 1-bit cutout, and exactly wrong for anti-aliased serifs and a soft shadow) and a LINEAR/CLAMP upload (NEAREST is for pixel-exact 320x200 art at integer scale; the logo is high-resolution at a non-integer one). Both are caller opt-ins - ui/titleScreen.js is the only caller and no game art path changed | Mac ("Let's tackle all of it. This is also our logo") |
| Main-menu EXIT button (U21) | DFU's Exit quits the application (DaggerfallStartWindow.cs:60). A browser tab cannot close itself unless script opened it. The button is PAINTED INTO PICK03I0 so it still draws, its verbatim rect still consumes the click, and it says so rather than pretending - it does not silently do nothing | Runtime difference (browser) |
| Main-menu HOTKEYS (U21) | DFU gives all three buttons rebindable Hotkey/OnKeyboardEvent arms (DaggerfallShortcut.Buttons.MainMenu*). Those are a DFU enhancement over classic, their default bindings live in a StreamingAssets table rather than in code, and this port has no keybinding registry to hang them on. Recorded rather than invented - the stance U20a took on ResetBonusPool. The menu is click-only, as classic's is | UI arc (a keybinding slice) |
| WebAudio playback engine (A1) | Unity AudioSource/3D audio -> WebAudio (PannerNode linear falloff, gesture-gated context, lazy 8-bit PCM decode). The DATA path stays 1:1: SoundClips indices, GetSwingSound pitch table, PlayHitSound roll families, EnemySounds attract shape (radius 16, delay 3..9, 80/20 bark/move, humans silent, attack 50%), door clips. |

PromotionNoHouseId (5241) : PromotionHouseId (5240)`. Banking is not ported, so WHICH of the two is genuinely unknown and stays flagged. But the port's fallback was 5237, the generic promotion record, and 5237 is the one record DFU picks in NEITHER branch: rank 9 is always 5240 or 5241. Rank 9 now takes 5240, the arm a player who does not already own a house gets, which is the common case and is wrong strictly less often than a record DFU never picks. When banking lands, PRESERVE DFU's ternary as written - it reads INVERTED (OwnsHouse -> "NoHouse") and this port is bug-for-bug | Best available answer under a routed gap **NARROWED 2026-08-26 (AUDIT 26 F114): the premise has expired.** Banking IS ported - `banking.js:167` ships ownsHouse and `worldModes.js:1694`, the very host that shows a promotion, already consumes it - so "which of the two is genuinely unknown" is no longer true and 5240 is no longer the best available answer, only the unwired one. Routed in section C; DFU's inverted ternary still stands as written when it lands. **LANDED 2026-08-27**: the section C row is struck - `promotionForRank(rank, ctx)` ships the ternary as written |
| getTitle answers NULL rather than the player's name for three guilds (AUDIT 21 F7) | Guild.GetTitle (:178-181) returns the player's NAME to a non-member, and the port's header cited that as the reason its one-line fallback was faithful. Returning the player's name there was a WRONG KNOWN value dressed as a withheld unknown one, so getTitle now answers null wherever DFU answers a string the port does not have. The four gendered rank slots (Temple 9 matriarch / 6 sister, KnightlyOrder 5 knightSister, DarkBrotherhood 8 darkSister) are STRUCTURE rather than localization and are recorded on the guild records | Native-window rule (text) |
| A FAILED POTION MIX MAKES NOTHING (`src/systems/potions.js`) | M1. This is DFU's departure, not the port's, and it is recorded here because the port INHERITS it: classic Daggerfall creates a useless "Unknown Powers" potion when the cauldron matches no recipe, and DaggerfallPotionMakerWindow.cs:330-332 refuses instead - with its own comment saying so and the classic line left commented out beside it. The port follows DFU rather than classic, so a failed mix burns the ingredients and yields an empty pot. The ingredients are spent either way in both. If the "Unknown Powers" bottle is ever wanted, only the failed arm of mixCauldron changes | Deliberate departure (DFU's own, inherited) |
| ~~THE TAVERN'S KEYBOARD ACCELERATORS ARE THE PORT'S OWN (`src/ui/tavernWindow.js`)~~ **RETIRED at D1 (2026-09-02)** | U39 said: DFU binds each of the four buttons to a `DaggerfallShortcut` hotkey (`:103-121`) "read from the player's own keybind file. That is a SECOND binding table, separate from the InputManager registry I1 ported, and the port has no source for it - so R/T/F/Esc here are the port's own". The second half was never true and the first half is now stale twice over: DaggerfallShortcut reads a TEXT DATABASE, `StreamingAssets/Text/DialogShortcuts.txt` (`DaggerfallShortcut.cs:307-326`), not a player keybind file - and A8 ported that file whole to `systems/dialogShortcuts.js`. D1 wires it through THREE windows at once: `ui/tavernWindow.js` (`TAVERN_BUTTONS`), `ui/covenWindow.js` (`COVEN_BUTTONS`) and `ui/guildServiceWindow.js`, each asking `firstHotkey` in DFU's own button ADD order. The invented letters were WRONG in three places, which is the cost of the departure being carried rather than checked: the tavern's Exit is G, not E; the coven's Summon is D, not S; and the guild popup has no single service letter at all - `Services.GetServiceShortcutButton` (`Services.cs:408-459`, ported as `guildServiceFlow.serviceShortcutButton`) moves it with the SERVICE, so the flat KeyS was right for two of the twenty. Escape/Enter stay the port host's own close keys, which is a host convention and not a DFU button. Every other law of the tavern window is DFU's verbatim, including the three closing quirks (food closes before it tests hunger, declining a price closes the tavern, the gold test is at the YES) | ~~Deliberate departure~~ CLOSED - the second binding table has a source and it is in the reference tree |

DaggerfallItemMakerWindow declares its own `itemListPanelRect` (10,0,50,148) with four 50x37 buttons, where every other native list in this port rides `ui/itemScroller.js` at (9,0,50,152) with four 50x38 - the geometry the 17e audit corrected once, for the icon margin, the ScaleToFit cap and the stack label's alignment. Riding it here puts the item maker's icons one pixel left and its rows one pixel taller than DFU's. One corrected scroller is worth the pixel; the alternative is a second copy of the same layout that the next audit has to fix twice. If the item maker ever needs its own metrics, they are two constants | Deliberate departure (one shared component over per-window geometry) |
| THE CROSSHAIR AND MODE ICON ARE DRAWN, NOT LOADED (`src/ui/hudCrosshair.js`) | U38. HUDCrosshair and HUDInteractionModeIcon both load DFU-AUTHORED PNGs out of Unity's Resources folder - "Crosshair", plus four icon sets of four files each (icon-/classic-/colour-/mono- x steal/grab/info/talk). None of it is ARENA2 data: it is DFU's own artwork, outside the C# this project translates and absent from the sparse clone, so there is nothing to read. The port draws a centred cross of its own geometry and shows the mode's NAME in the HUD font. Every LAW around them IS ported verbatim - the cursor-active suppression, the position right of the vitals bars on their baseline, resScale's divisor, and the xhair-suffix branch where the indicator replaces the crosshair for three modes while Grab keeps it - because the law is the half the source actually carries. If DFU's icon art is ever vendored under its MIT licence, only the two draw calls change | Deliberate departure (DFU-authored art the port cannot read) |

DFU lets the strip overlap a long final line even though the label block reserved exactly stripH + 4 beneath it - the race descriptions all end with one, and it reads "Is your [YES]ter to [NO]Redguard?". The port clamps `stripY = Math.max(stripY, textY + textH + BUTTON_TEXT_DISTANCE)`. This is the sharpest of the four F8 rows because it is a DFU bug the port chose NOT to be bug-for-bug about, which is exactly the class of decision section A exists to record - and it had been declared only at its own site | Deliberate departure (a DFU layout bug not reproduced) |
| SPELLS.STD: a failed record ALWAYS resyncs to the record boundary (`src/formats/spellsStd.js`) | AUDIT 21 (doctrine lane, F8). DFU's `ReadSpellData` returns false having consumed 6 of 89 bytes and never seeks to the boundary, so its loop resumes 83 bytes early and walks into the middle of the next record. The port always resyncs to `start + SPELL_RECORD_SIZE`. INERT on shipping data - no classic record fails the gate - so this is a divergence that cannot be observed on the user's own files, recorded so nobody "restores" DFU's desync | Deliberate departure (inert on classic data) |

(e.g. #91)" - so a throw is EXPECTED traffic in DFU, not an error. The port returns null: same control flow, without an exception on a routine path every scene host would have to guard. Verified equivalent on all 63 ggroup-9/17 factions that are neither a Divine, a templar order nor one of the ten Orders | Approved (behaviourally identical) |

DFU's FactionData_v2 serialises the whole dictionary; the port saves the columns that can change and re-reads the rest from the user's own FACTION.TXT on load. Same semantics, because the immutable columns cannot differ - and it keeps a save from carrying a private copy of game data, which the doctrine's first non-negotiable would otherwise have something to say about | Approved (same state, smaller record) |

Same observable behaviour, expressed the only way a pure fold can express it. On the real corpus 10 of the 2,980 Temple buildings carry factionId 0 and reach this line | Approved (pure-function shape; behaviour identical) |

JS objects are references, so assigning across would let a reputation change reach into the shared reader and follow one character into the next. createFactionRep clones. Same semantics; the only way to get them | Approved |

STILL OURS (the presentation split every screen here takes): WHERE values live - DFU writes an ini beside the executable, a browser has none, so the store persists a DELTA against the defaults in localStorage; and the TIERS, which are a claim about THIS port, not about DFU - `live` (a consumer exists: CombatVoices, PlayerTorchFromItems, LoiterLimitInHours, SoundVolume, MusicVolume, MouseLookSensitivity, InvertMouseVertical), `stored` (round-trips, no consumer yet - the INTERIM doctrine), `unavailable` (meaningless here, or the port implements ONE side of the branch). **THE DIVERGENCE THE OLD ROW EXISTED TO NAME SURVIVES AND IS NOW ENFORCED: `EnhancedCombatAI` is SHIPPED TRUE by DFU and this port implements the classic FALSE path** - enemy FOV 180 not 190 (EnemySenses.cs:239-241), no strafing, no backing away, no retreat roll, `TargetRateOfApproach` identically zero (:354-363) - so it is tiered `unavailable` WITH that reason on screen rather than offered as a toggle that would lie. `AdvancedClimbing` is the same shape. settings.test.js re-derives the live set from the code, so a tier that lies fails the suite (it caught two of mine while this row was being written) | Recorded 2026-08-21, built the same day at Mac's "develop our own launcher and implement the settings" |

| ~~X11b: Identify REFUSES outside an interior instead of opening anywhere~~ RETIRED THE NEXT LANE (`src/scenes/worldModes.js`) | Identify can be cast anywhere in DFU. This host's three spell-window openers all wrote into `interiorOverlay`, which it draws in INTERIOR mode only - so an outdoor or in-dungeon cast mounted a window nothing drew, ticked or clicked, and the magicka was gone. X11b routed the two PICKERS through a per-mode slot-picker and left Identify refusing, because its window is a TRADE window whose lifecycle was entangled with a MODULE-LEVEL `_identifySpell` latch drained in four separate `interiorOverlay?.done` arms. **X11c 2026-08-25 closed it**: the latch moved onto the commit closure `openTradeWindow` builds per window, so its lifetime is the window's and no slot has to remember it; the four drains and the module state are gone; `openIdentifyWindow` mounts through the same slot-picker as the other two, and the `mode !== 'interior'` refusal with it. The live probe casts Identify in the STREET and drives the window to a completed deal. ~~One residue, ROUTED not swallowed: the STANDALONE dungeon host (`src/scenes/dungeonContext.js`, the `?dungeon` dev route) has no trade window at all - no `NativeTradeWindow`, no trade art - so Identify there still has nowhere to mount.~~ PR1 (2026-08-27) closed HALF the residue: the host was in fact SILENT there (the absent seams optional-chained past the engine's dispatch), and both `onIdentify`/`onDispelMagic` were made to refuse out loud. ~~The trade window itself was never owed, DFU having no standalone dungeon scene.~~ **DR1 (2026-09-03) retired that reason and shipped both windows there** (section C's row, struck): "never owed" was true of where the window was BUILT and false as a refusal - `NativeTradeWindow` and `ListPickerWindow` are `isChoiceWindow` natives over host-agnostic art, and `dungeonContext.js` already drove every seam one needs (`pushDungeonWindow`, `tickOverlay`, `overlayClick`/`overlayPointer`/`overlayHover`/`overlayWheel`, `drawOverlay`'s native arm). Both now open and complete on the `?dungeon` route; what is left of the loud arm is the ART guard every host carries. | X11b 2026-08-25, RETIRED X11c 2026-08-25 |
| T2: SOLD GOODS LAND ON THE OPEN SHELF, where DFU simply discards them (`src/scenes/worldModes.js`) | ConfirmTrade's Sell arm ends `remoteItems.Clear()` (:1051) - the goods the merchant just bought cease to exist, and a player who sells a sword by mistake cannot buy it back at any price. The port pushes them onto the shelf the window is already showing, so the sale is reversible at the merchant's own buy price. E3 shipped this deliberately in 2026-08-17 ("proceeds via addGold with sold goods landing on the OPEN shelf (buy-backs work)") and the reasoning is recorded in Home.md's E3 note and UI-Arc.md, but it never reached Ledger A - found while reading ConfirmTrade line by line for the letter-of-credit announcement. It costs nothing in fidelity that a player can observe except that the shelf grows, and shelves restock lazily anyway. | E3 2026-08-17, RECORDED T2 2026-08-25 |
| T1: the torch's GUTTERING rides RANGE, not intensity (`src/systems/playerTorch.js`) | EnablePlayerTorch cycles an INTENSITY band under condition 3 - `0.85 + cos(guttering) * 0.2`, walked by `Random.Range(-0.02, 0.06)`. This port's point-light channel is a vec4 `[x, y, z, range]` with ONE colour per scene; there is no per-light intensity to put it in. DFU itself animates RANGE for every scene light (DaggerfallLight.AnimateLight, ported verbatim in `world/worldClock.js`), so range IS this port's light-animation channel, and the band is expressed in it - normalised against the steady 1.25 so a healthy torch is exactly its template radius. The visible law survives: the light pulses as the fuel runs out. `PlayerTorchLightScale` stays inert for the opposite reason - it is a 0..1 BRIGHTNESS whose default of 1.0 is a no-op, and mapping a brightness slider onto a radius would be a worse lie than leaving it alone | T1 2026-08-25 |
| C2: the automap RESTORE GUARD refuses the whole save, not a prefix (`src/systems/automapModel.js`) | RestoreStateAutomapDungeon's layout guard (Automap.cs:2378-2386) `return`s MID-WALK after `HideAll()` the moment a block name disagrees with the saved one, leaving every block BEFORE the mismatch restored - so a re-ordered layout restores a prefix of the WRONG models. The port answers the layout question BEFORE applying anything (`restoreMatchesLayout`), so a mismatch restores NOTHING. The port's discovery keys are position-independent, which makes a partial restore strictly worse than none: it would mark models revealed the player never saw and leave no way to tell. Same intent - a re-ordered layout must not restore wrong discovery - stricter answer | C2-S1 2026-09-01 (deliberate, recorded at the site) |
| C2: a mouse RELEASE ends a panel drag WHEREVER it lands (`src/ui/automapChrome.js`) | DFU's PanelAutomap_OnMouseUp is a component event, so releasing OUTSIDE the render panel leaves `leftMouseDownOnPanelAutomap` set and the map keeps dragging - a real latch in the original. The port routes `up` from the WINDOW for exactly this reason and clears the drag on any release: an unreleasable drag is worse than a one-frame difference in where the drag ends | C2-S4 2026-09-01 (deliberate, recorded at the site) |
| ~~C2: the dungeon automap's HELD hotkeys repeat at the browser's key-repeat rate, not once per frame (`src/ui/automapWindow.js`)~~ RETIRED (E-group, 2026-09-02): the seam the row named as the blocker is built. `ui/input.js`'s `routeKeyUp` mirrors `routeKey`, and the key UP now reaches the overlay slot in every host that owns one - `scenes/townTalk.js`'s `keyup`, `scenes/worldModes.js`'s `keyup` in both modes, `scenes/dungeonContext.js`'s `overlayKeyUp` (fed by `worldModes` and `scenes/dungeon.js`), `scenes/interior.js`'s own listener, with `world.js`/`exterior.js` delivering into both slots they hold. Both automap windows keep InputManager's held-key dictionary (`_heldCodes`, with GetKeyboardKeyModifiers' own two-source modifier word) and poll the IsPressedWith class once per FRAME in `tick()` - `_tickHeldHotkeys`, a flat chain like DFU's, so two held keys both run on one frame - and the two-phase toggle-close closes on DFU's own `GetKeyUp` (:707-713) rather than draining a frame late. THE SAME EDGE reached the two other windows this row's own citation named as carrying the gap: `ui/restWindow.js`'s toggle close is Update :193's `GetKeyUp(toggleClosedBinding) || GetBackButtonUp()` and StopButton's keyboard twin is its real two phases (:714-726, ButtonClick and the latch on the down, EndRest on the up) with the MOUSE click left as the one call it is (:708-712); `ui/pauseWindow.js`'s Escape close is :183-188's `GetKeyUp`. Those two windows also take the DEFERRAL DFU gives every window whose OPEN edge is the key down (`DaggerfallAutomapWindow.cs:703-713`'s `isCloseWindowDeferred`), because that is this port's open edge in all four hosts while DFU's is the release: `GameManager.cs:534-537` and :515-518 post both windows through `ActionComplete`, which `InputManager.cs:634-637` defines as the UP edge, so DFU's opening release is spent before the window exists and a bare `GetKeyUp` is safe there and is not here. Armed by a press the window itself saw, consumed by that press's release - so the R that opens the rest window, and the Escape that opens the pause screen, cannot close them on their own release. With the split real, the `HotkeySequenceProcessed == NotFound` gate (:189) needs no port: the buttons are read on the press and the toggle on the release, so a colliding binding resolves DFU's way by construction. Pinned by `test/roade_up_seam.test.js`, `test/roadb_rest_residue.test.js`, `test/a8_shortcuts.test.js` and `test/pausewindow.test.js`; the four retired sentences are swept out of `src/` by `test/flagsweep.test.js`'s FS1. | DaggerfallAutomapWindow's Update polls twenty-two movement/rotate/slice/zoom hotkeys with `HotkeySequence.IsPressedWith`, which is `InputManager.GetKey` - HELD, every frame - which is why every speed they carry is per-SECOND. The port's overlay key seam delivers key DOWNS only (`ui/input.js` routeKey; `ui/restWindow.js` records the same gap for its toggle close), so holding an automap hotkey advances the camera at the OS auto-repeat rate instead. The MOUSE half of the identical verbs IS parity - `ui/automapChrome.js` keeps its own down-flags and `tick(dt)` polls them once per frame - so only the keyboard is approximate, and the same seam is what defers the toggle-close by a frame rather than closing on the press DFU closes on the RELEASE of. Closing it means routing key-up through the four hosts, which is a seam change, not a window change | C2-S5 2026-09-01 (deliberate, recorded at the site) |
| C2: the automap's WIREFRAME mode is `gl.LINES` over an edge index buffer, not a geometry shader (`src/render/renderer.js`) | DaggerfallAutomap.shader's second pass runs a GEOMETRY SHADER (`[maxvertexcount(3)] geom`) that hands each fragment the triangle's barycentric distances; the fragment then clips everything with `exp2(-4*d*d) < 0.1` and writes a CONSTANT colour on what survives. WebGL2 has no geometry stage, so the port expands each triangle into its three edges (a shared edge therefore appears twice, exactly as DFU's per-triangle barycentrics draw it) and issues `gl.LINES`. THE LOSS IS SMALL AND IS STATED AS SUCH: because DFU hard-clips at I &lt; 0.1 and writes a flat colour, there is no soft falloff to lose - only a ~0.9 px hard band that becomes a 1 px line (WebGL2 caps `lineWidth` at 1 on every desktop driver). Quad diagonals are not a delta at all; DFU shows them too. Deliberately NOT closed with a barycentric vertex variant: that needs a de-indexed copy of every automap-eligible mesh (three unique vertices per triangle) and doubles their vertex memory to buy under a pixel. The line buffer is built lazily, once per mesh, over the mesh's OWN vertex buffers, and is freed with it | C2-S6 2026-09-01 (deliberate, recorded at the site) |
| C2: the automap draws GROUP BY GROUP where DFU draws object by object (`src/ui/automapWindow.js`) | Unity queues both of the automap shader's passes per RENDERER, so DFU interleaves each object's below-slice and above-slice draw. The port issues four groups instead - below-slice colour, below-slice grayscale, then the above-slice pair - because the port's automap is a filter over the host's own draw lists and a per-object flip would re-upload the mode uniform per model. The two differ ONLY where an above-slice transparent fragment would have blended before a LATER object's below-slice depth write. That is inside DFU's own tolerance rather than outside it: the above-slice pass is `Blend SrcAlpha OneMinusSrcAlpha` with `ZWrite On` and NO queue sorting, so DFU's output is already order-dependent by construction - the artifacts ARE the classic look and are the target, not a defect either implementation avoids | C2-S6 2026-09-01 (deliberate, recorded at the site) |
| C2: the automap's ROTATION-INDICATOR ARROW is procedural (`src/ui/automapMarkers.js`) | SetupBeacons instantiates `Resources.Load("RotateArrow")` twice under the rotation pivot axis (Automap.cs:1392, :1402) - a Unity prefab over `Assets/Resources/RotateArrows.obj`, a 576-vertex model AUTHORED FOR DAGGERFALL UNITY that is not Daggerfall game data and so cannot come through this port's data door at all. The port builds a flat curved arrow of its own and places it at DFU's exact transform: the same parent (so the pivot's (0.15, 50.2, 0.15) multiplies both the +/-(2, -0.02, -2) offsets and the (0.15, 0.0005, 0.15) scale, landing them 0.3 units out at 0.0225 wide), the same blue material, the same 180-degree turn on the second one. What is lost is the authored silhouette of a ~3 cm decal; what is kept is every number the C# states. The alternative - vendoring a third-party .obj - would put a non-Daggerfall art asset in the repo for a mark smaller than the beacon it labels | Automap.cs SetupBeacons (:1390-1412) | C2-S7 2026-09-01 (deliberate, recorded at the site) |
| C2: the automap PICKER tests marker BOXES where DFU raycasts their colliders (`src/systems/automapPick.js`) | `GetRayCastNearestHitOnAutomapLayer` (:1840-1856) is `Physics.RaycastAll` over real colliders: `CreatePrimitive` leaves a CapsuleCollider on each of the three cylinder beacons (the `Destroy(GetComponent<Collider>())` beside them is commented out at :1367, :1382 and :1421), a BoxCollider on the entrance cube, and `AddComponent<MeshCollider>()` on the player arrow and the two curved arrows. The port hit-tests each marker's ORIENTED BOX instead. The delta is stated at its true size: for the beacons it is a 0.3-wide box corner against a 0.15-radius capsule over a 100-unit ray - under a pixel at every zoom the map offers - and the cube's box IS its collider; only the arrows are genuinely coarser, and their whole job is to answer one status-bar string. The LEVEL GEOMETRY is not part of this substitution: it is intersected triangle by triangle against the same CPU data the host drew, filtered by `MeshRenderer.enabled` (:1850) exactly as the C# filters | Automap.GetRayCastNearestHitOnAutomapLayer / SetupBeacons | C2-S7 2026-09-01 (deliberate, recorded at the site) |
| C2: the automap's TELEPORTER KEY carries a yaw-built quaternion where DFU's carries the exit Transform's own (`src/systems/automap.js`) | `TeleporterConnection.ToString()` (Automap.cs:130-137) is `"position: " + teleporterEntrance.position + ", rotation: " + teleporterExit.rotation` - the ENTRANCE position (already carrying its +up*1.0 offset, applied in TeleporterTransform's constructor) mixed with the EXIT rotation. That string is not decoration: it is the dictionary key, the name of both marker GameObjects, and - through `AutomapDungeonState.dictTeleporterConnections` - THE SAVE KEY, so its bytes are a persistence contract. DFU reads `nextObj.transform.rotation`, a full quaternion. The port's action objects carry a YAW ALONE (`world/dungeonLayout.js`'s `objectPositions` records `yawDeg`, which is all an RDB object has), so the port formats `Quaternion.Euler(0, yaw, 0)` into the same four F1 slots. For every teleporter Daggerfall actually ships the two agree, because RDB action objects are yaw-only; a hypothetical pitched exit would key differently. The FORMAT is byte-identical - Unity 2019.4's `Vector3.ToString()` / `Quaternion.ToString()` are `"({0:F1}, ...)"`, and the port reproduces .NET's F1 including its half-away-from-zero rounding and its `"-0.0"`, which JS `toFixed` gets wrong on negative ties. Both halves are derived from the block's STATIC layout data rather than from an accumulated runtime transform, and quantised to one decimal before they are ever compared, so the key round-trips through a save and a re-walked portal resolves to the entry it already has | Automap.TeleporterConnection.ToString / OnTeleportAction (:2565-2572) | C2-S8 2026-09-01 (deliberate, recorded at the site) |
| C2: interior-building discovery is VISIT-SCOPED and never enters the dungeon dictionary (`src/systems/automap.js`) | `SaveStateAutomapDungeon` (Automap.cs:2131-2239) runs on every `GetState` and only returns early when the player is OUTSIDE (:2139-2143). Inside a BUILDING `gameobjectGeometry` holds the INTERIOR copy, so a save taken in a shop walks that hierarchy - the "Models"/"Doors" nodes as blocks - writes it into `dictAutomapDungeonsDiscoveryState` under the TOWN's `"RegionName/Name"` with the interior scene name as its `locationName`, and then runs the LRU prune (:2216-2238) over the result. Two consequences follow in DFU and neither is intended: a shop's map can EVICT a real dungeon's under `AutomapNumberOfDungeons`, and it SHADOWS that town's dungeon record until the dungeon is next entered (whereupon the locationName mismatch at :2381 discards it). Reload in the same building and :2381 does match, so interior discovery accidentally survives that one round trip. The port keeps the interior record OUT of that dictionary entirely: `enterInteriorAutomap` mints a session object that is never snapshot, never restored, and whose `int:<n>` keys never enter the save's `${bi}:${position}` space. Every OTHER path already makes interior discovery per-visit - the same `locationName` guard ends the restore (:2381), and `RestoreStateAutomapInterior` (:2280-2305), which is the only function that would have read the interior's own saved state, HAS NO CALLER ANYWHERE in the reference and is deliberately not ported - so this departure applies that same per-visit answer to one more door and costs no behaviour a player can otherwise reach | Automap.SaveStateAutomapDungeon / RestoreStateAutomapDungeon (:2131-2239, :2351-2422) | C2-S9 2026-09-01 (deliberate, recorded at the site) |
| T1: the torch's OFFSET is read as LOCAL, not as the world write DFU makes (`src/systems/playerTorch.js`) | `torchLight.transform.position = new Vector3(-0.3f, 1.2f, 0.2f)` runs ONCE in Start (:52). PlayerTorch is a CHILD of the player and `transform.position` is a WORLD write, so what that line actually fixes is a local offset of `(-0.3, 1.2, 0.2) minus wherever the player happened to be standing when Start ran - and the torch rides that arbitrary offset for the whole session. With the setting OFF the line never runs at all. It is not reproducible and it is plainly not what the numbers mean, so the port reads them as the local offset they describe: a light at the player's left hand, 1.2 up, 0.2 forward, on a YAW-ONLY basis because the player body turns in yaw while the pitch belongs to the camera | T1 2026-08-25 |
| TB1: TEXT ENTRY IS AN INLINE BOX (or strip) THE HOST WINDOW DRAWS, not a `DaggerfallInputMessageBox` pushed on the UI stack (`src/ui/spellMakerWindow.js`, `src/ui/itemMakerWindow.js`, `src/ui/spellbookWindow.js`, `src/ui/travelMapWindow.js`, `src/ui/automapWindow.js`) | DFU opens a SEPARATE WINDOW for every prompt: `NameSpellButton_OnMouseClick` builds `new DaggerfallInputMessageBox(uiManager, this)`, seeds `mb.TextBox.Text`, sets the label and `Show()`s it (DaggerfallSpellMakerWindow.cs:911-918; `EnterName_OnGotUserInput` at :1034 takes the result), and the item maker's rename (DaggerfallItemMakerWindow.cs:799-811), the spellbook's rename, the travel map's find (DaggerfallTravelMapWindow.cs:965) and the automap's note (DaggerfallAutomapWindow.cs:1594-1607) are the same shape. The port draws the prompt and the typed line INSIDE the host window - `layoutMessageBox`/`drawMessageBox` over the window's own art, or the label with a trailing `_` where the field IS the label - and the host's own key router owns the keystrokes: `spellMakerWindow.js`'s `naming`/`_nameInput`, `itemMakerWindow.js`'s `renaming`, `spellbookWindow.js`'s `renameText`, `travelMapWindow.js`'s `findText`, `automapWindow.js`'s `_noteBox`/`_noteBoxInput`. There is no window MANAGER here to push onto; these windows are DRAWN, not instantiated. What the box IS stays faithful: the same prompt record, the same field width, and `TextBox`'s own `maxCharacters` default of 31 (TextBox.cs:26) wherever DFU never narrows it - 50 for the automap note (:1603), 32 for find (:968) | ROAD-E fix-D, 2026-09-02. Written to CARRY a departure the sites had already been declaring: `ui/spellMakerWindow.js` and `ui/itemMakerWindow.js` both said "Ledger A carries the widget row already" and section A carried no such row at all - the AUDIT 17m / F7 shape, a claim of approval standing in for one. The idiom is approved as written; the ROW is the part that was missing |
| **THE WIND (WIND1, 2026-09-02)** - `systems/wind.js`. Daggerfall has NO wind: DFU's WeatherManager carries no such number, so there is no original this can be wrong against. The enhanced sky's per-weather wind vectors were already a departure (ES1); this replaces them with a model - a per-day calm, a front at every weather change that leads the sky by three hours and rolls away over two, and gusts shaped by strength - feeding WM2b's one seam. The weather SIM is untouched: it still cuts once a day and on climate crossings, as DFU does, and the front is that cut. ENHANCED ONLY; the classic sky never reads the row. | Mac's call, 2026-09-02 ("wind should be something different from the weather... a storm rolling in as the wind kicks up"), recorded in `07-Rendering/Rendering.md` WIND1 | WIND1 |
| **READER-SIDE BOUNDED STRING SCANS (road/maps-record, 2026-09-03)** - `src/formats/bsaFile.js` and `src/formats/mapsFile.js`. DFU finds a NUL with `FileProxy.ReadCString(reader, 0)` (FileProxy.cs:380-391), an UNBOUNDED `reader.ReadByte()` loop, so a string that runs to the end of its buffer throws EndOfStreamException. Two scans here CLAMP instead. `BsaFile`'s NameRecord directory scan stops at the 14-byte name field, where BsaFile.cs:400 would read on into the Int32 RecordSize (the size still decodes - ReadDirectory seeks back to Position + 14 - but the NAME would carry those bytes, and on the last entry the scan runs off the end of the file). `MapsFile._readCStringSkip` stops at the end of the MAPNAMES record, where DFU's throw propagates to ReadRegion's catch and LoadRegion returns false for the WHOLE region. Neither clamp can fire on classic data, measured on a real MAPS.BSA: all 248 directory names are the 12-byte `MAPXITEM.NNN` form, and of the 15,251 name slots exactly two fill their 32-byte stride (Bhoriane 1956..2009 of 6084, Kambria 8644..8701 of 9924), both stopping far short of their record end. Two more MapsFile departures ride this row because they are the same reader and the same sweep: `loadRegion` MEMOIZES where C# re-reads the four BSA records every call (identical decoded data, and autoDiscard's one-resident-region invariant still holds), and `getRegion` / `getRegionByName` / `getLocation` / `getLocationByName` return NULL where C# returns a default-constructed DFRegion/DFLocation with `Loaded = false` (port-wide convention; every consumer already guards with `?.` / `?? null`) | A refusal to crash on data classic does not contain, and a decode byte-identical wherever DFU would not throw. Each departure is stated at its own site instead of being implied by a 'verbatim' header, and pinned both ways - source pins with no ARENA2, MAPS-only data pins with it - in `test/road_maps_record.test.js` | Port-Doctrine (a crash is not a behaviour worth reproducing); road/maps-record 2026-09-03 |

## A-note (H1): the houses-for-sale seed

DFU rolls the town's house market with

    Random.InitState(buildingDict.GetHashCode() + Now.Month)

and `GetHashCode()` on a Dictionary is the CLR's OBJECT-IDENTITY hash -
a different number every time the process runs. So the `+ Month` term,
which plainly intends "the same houses are for sale all month", is
defeated by the term it is added to: in DFU the market reshuffles on
every load. A JS port cannot reproduce a CLR identity hash and should
not want to, so `banking.housesForSale` seeds on the LOCATION and the
month - what that expression was reaching for. Pinned both ways in
`test/houses.test.js`: stable inside a month, turned over between them,
and different per town.

## B. Verbatim quirks preserved (real-data reality)

World:
- THE GREEN "MARKER / START" PLACARD OUTSIDE PRIVATEER'S HOLD. The first-hour
  probe's screenshot of the world, taken the moment the player steps out of
  the starting dungeon, has an editor marker standing in the grass. It is
  real data and it is faithfully drawn: `CUSTAA30.RMB` - the ONE block of
  the Privateer's Hold exterior - carries exactly one misc flat, and it is
  archive **199 record 10**, the editor START marker (read off the shipped
  BLOCKS.BSA, not inferred). DFU draws it too, as far as its source can be
  read: `RMBLayout.AddMiscBlockFlats` (:340-379) skips only the LIGHTS
  archive and hands everything else to
  `GameObjectHelper.CreateDaggerfallBillboardGameObject` (:313), which has no
  archive-199 branch either - the 199 filter sits in `AddExteriorBlockFlats`
  (:409, the building SUBRECORD loop) and in RDBLayout, both of which the
  port already mirrors (`world/rmbFlats.js:85`, `world/rdbLayout.js:357`).
  So the port's misc-flat loop matches DFU's line for line, and NOT filtering
  here is the faithful reading. FLAGGED, and honestly: what has been checked
  is the data and both sources; what has NOT been checked is DFU actually
  rendering it on screen, which needs a Unity build nobody here can run. If a
  side-by-side ever shows DFU's exterior clean, this row moves to a defect
  and `collectBlockFlats`' first loop gains the same guard its second one has.

Chargen:
- `characterDocument.isCustom` is assigned in exactly ONE place in the whole
  DFU codebase (`= true` on the Custom row,
  DaggerfallStartNewGameWizard.cs:358) and is never cleared. So opening the
  custom builder, cancelling out of it
  and then picking a standard class still ships a document marked custom: it
  gets the custom starting kit (ItemHelper.cs:1310) and the Spellsword spell
  set (StartGameBehaviour.cs:804-809), so a Mage picked that way starts with
  the Spellsword spells. The five reputation fields ride the same rule
  (:385-389, written only in the builder's accept arm). Reproduced
  bug-for-bug in ui/chargen.js `_acceptStandardClass`.
  (Added at AUDIT 18: that function's comment already said "recorded in the
  Ledger" and no such row existed - the 17m shape exactly.)

Characters:
- ENEMY BOOTS never reduce ArmorValues[Feet]. EnemyEntity.SetEnemyEquipment's
  armour pass is `for (int i = (int)EquipSlots.Head; i < (int)EquipSlots.Feet;
  i++)` (EnemyEntity.cs:414) - a STRICT `<` against Feet, and Head = 12 /
  Feet = 26, so slot 26 is never visited. DFU still rolls and equips the
  boots (ItemHelper.cs:1452-1457) and they still drop as corpse loot; only
  the armour-value pass skips them, leaving ArmorValues[Feet] at 100 before
  the class/monster clamp. To preserve it the port must skip Boots in the
  value loop ONLY - never in the roll, which consumes a Dice100 and a
  material draw from the stream. (Added at AUDIT 18, which found
  combat/enemyEquipment.js:131-139 subtracting boots there while its header
  called the pass verbatim: at plate steel that is Feet 55 against DFU's 60,
  and at daedric -5 against 60, a 65-point swing straight into
  calculateSuccessfulHit. The code fix is routed to the Combat lane of the
  same audit.)
- RandomEncounters.cs:580-599 carries a commented-out second Cemetery table
  ("Cemetery - DF Unity version"); the generated encounterTables.js is
  comment-stripped, so the port ships the 45 LIVE tables and not that dead
  block. (Added at AUDIT 18 - encounterTables.js:5 cited "Ledger B" for it
  and there was no row.)
- SENT7.RMB carries one archive-210 lamp flat with factionID -4080 (Int16): DFU's faction != 0 rule makes the lamp a StaticNPC; reproduced verbatim (C2 corpus pin).
- NameGen.txt ships with two lenient-JSON constructs inside Monster3 (a missing comma, a trailing comma); FullSerializer treats commas as optional, so the committed normalized nameGen.json equals what DFU loads.

Readers:
- BSA: junk record 669 "FOO" in BLOCKS.BSA; structural closure invariant.
- BLOCKS vs the C#, MEASURED at AUDIT 18 by running DaggerfallConnect's own
  BlocksFile under Mono over the whole corpus: 5,411,170 values compared,
  NINE differ, and all nine are inert and accounted for. (a) Record 669 (the
  junk "FOO") - DFU's ReadBlock falls through its else and returns a
  populated-but-empty DFBlock; the port returns null. Every call site guards
  it (world.js, locationLayout.js). (b) FixRdbData's three SYNTHESIZED
  objects in blocks 1025/1034/1036 - C# leaves ActionResource.
  PreviousObjectOffset and NextObjectIndex at the struct default 0 where the
  port's defaultActionResource() uses -1. Dead data: those objects carry
  Flags = 0, so HasAction is false and RDBLayout.AddActionModelHelper - the
  only reader of PreviousObjectOffset - never runs for them. Before AUDIT 18
  fixed the fixed-length ReadCString this count was 262,589.
- Palettes: MAP.PAL filename triggers x4 six-bit expansion.
- TEXTURE: archives 215/217/436 unsupported (as DFU); wild compression values
  0x900/0x101/0x100 fall through to the uncompressed default; getColor32
  vertical flip and double alpha assignment kept.
- IMG: 6 palettized files read their embedded palette; FMAP0I00/01/16
  unsupported; ImgFile reads raw w*h regardless of the compression field.
- CIF: WEAPON09 (bow) has no wield record; FACES.CIF routes as RCI 64x64.
- ARCH3D: UV unpack only first 3 points AND recordId < 905 with the -7168 ->
  1024 exception; 905-entry patch table applied to a working copy; fixed
  buffers throw on overflow exactly like the C# arrays; min/max seeded at 0
  so Size spans the origin.
- BLOCKS: RMB subrecords step by blockDataSizes, not bytes read; RDB unknown
  list sized by the FIRST node's index; object root must start at the header
  offset; flat factionOrMobileId re-reads two bytes as LE u16; "REF_CUBE"
  and 0xff model ids parse to 0 via strict TryParse semantics; FixRdbData
  repairs blocks 994, 945/946, 958, 975, 1025, 1034, 1036; 8 RDBs carry
  0xff padding instead of the DAGR signature.
- MAPS: 17 regions ship empty records and are rejected (45 populated, as
  classic); two 32-byte names truncate exactly (Porcupine Hostel/Bhoriane,
  Feather and Barbarian/Kambria); letter2 = LETTER2[((2*char)&0xff)>>6], the
  8-bit truncation is load-bearing; LocationType decode uses uint
  wraparound; Orsinium (locationId 50015) border block moved to Z=-2.
- SND: record index 5 is zero-length; synthesized 44-byte RIFF header is
  byte-exact.
- WOODS: GetHeightMapValue clamps both axes with `>= dim - 1`;
  GetLargeHeightMapValuesRange strips the 5x5 to the interior 3x3 with
  INVERTED sample Y (src[ix][4 - iy]) and walks source pixels with a
  DESCENDING map Y (mapPixelY - y).

World layout:
- Exterior subrecord FLATS use the unrotated (subX, 0, -subZ) offset - DFU
  does not rotate flats with the building subrecord.
- Climate-swapped nature flats get `billboardPosition.z = natureFlatsOffsetY`
  (raw -2): suspected upstream y/z typo in RMBLayout.cs, kept verbatim.
  SETTLED: a corpus scan (pinned in world.test.js) shows zero exterior
  subrecord flats in archives 500-511 across all 920 RMB blocks - the branch
  is a dead code path on classic data and only fires for mod-injected
  buildings. Verbatim is provably identical to any fix.
- Ground tiles read [x][15 - y]; scenery skips records < 1; tile records
  >= 56 reset to grass (DFU's tilemap index 8 == texture record 2);
  offsets propsOffsetY -4, blockFlatsOffsetY -6,
  natureFlatsOffsetY -2; classic data never sets model scale.
- City LIGHTS (archive 210) place at (-Y + size.y) * GlobalScale where
  size.y is GetScaledBillboardSize's NATIVE height (MeshReader.cs:549-568
  applies no GlobalScale), and the light Y differs from the billboard's -6
  offset by design.
- ModelDoor extraction: door Index resets per submesh (DFU's doorCount
  scope); archive 156 (Scourg exterior) only exempts the base-archive
  reduction and never becomes a door; ruin-enter 331 record 0 is plain
  stone, skipped.
- On-terrain locations take NO RMB ground plane: StreamingWorld creates
  city blocks with addGroundPlane = false, so the stamped terrain tilemap
  is the ground and marker tiles (record >= 56) fall through to generated
  terrain tiles. Isolated-block scenes keep the ground plane (DFU default
  true).
- RDB flat positions are billboard CENTERS: AddFlat performs no
  AlignToBase, unlike the RMB exterior (AlignToBase) and interior
  (+size.y/2) paths which are base-anchored. Renderer-side base batches
  shift down half the scaled height.
- RDB model matrix composes T * Rz * Rx * Ry - a different rotation order
  than RMB's TRS (Ry * Rx * Rz). Red-brick doors (72100) carry the DOR tag
  but are never action doors. Flat actions pass the flat's MAGNITUDE as
  the raw axis. TRP objects with raw axis 13 rotate NegativeX at 400 (DFU
  bumps classic's 392 so the player cannot stick). LID/WHE force fixed
  rotations. RemoveOverlappingDoors seeds the exit-door centre WITHOUT the
  block origin (benign: start blocks sit at grid 0,0).
- Interiors: prop models (ObjectType 3) keep +Y UNNEGATED then anchor to
  the model's lowest vertex Y; IsBadInteriorModel repairs classic data by
  filtering misplaced model 31000 at 60 block/record combos across 27
  blocks (kept verbatim, like FixRdbData); action doors pin to the 9000
  model range via DoorModelIndex % 5 (900x..980x duplicates have differing
  origins); editor flats (199) are spawned as data but hidden from render.
- Rest (16f audit): the sub-tick interval is waitTimePerHour /
  minutesPerTick - DFU divides by the CONSTANT 10, not the 6 ticks an
  hour takes, so a rested hour passes in 0.45 real seconds (loiter
  0.75). Preserved verbatim (restSession.js).
  AUDIT 18 deleted this row's second sentence, which claimed a 0-hour
  timed/loiter request rests ONE full hour in DFU "because hoursRemaining
  < 1 is only tested after an hour completes". That is backwards:
  DaggerfallRestWindow.Update (:215-227) is an else-if ladder whose
  `(currentRestMode != RestModes.FullRest) && hoursRemaining < 1` arm sits
  AHEAD of `TickRest()`, so DFU ends a 0-hour rest on the very next Update
  with the clock unmoved. The row was recording a port divergence as a
  preserved quirk, which is what kept it from being audited; the code fix
  is routed to the Systems lane of the same audit (restSession.tick must
  test `mode !== 'full' && hoursRemaining < 1` before the sub-tick loop).
- Falling into deep water keeps the fall LIVE (P14): swimming never
  grounds, so wading out can bill the whole drop as fall damage -
  nothing in DFU clears it (AcrobatMotor.Falling untouched by
  LevitateMotor); preserved bug-for-bug.
- CONJURED ITEMS ARE A DFU RESTORATION, NOT A CLASSIC BEHAVIOUR (X11b).
  ItemCollection.RemoveExpiredItems carries its own note: "Reverse-
  engineering suggests this was intended behavior in classic, but
  classic does not correctly set the item flags so magically-created
  items never disappear." So in the original game a Create Item spell
  gives you permanent gear, and in DFU it does not. The port follows
  DFU, as the doctrine says - the whole conjured-item family (the
  lifetime, the sweep, the stacking split, the transfer refusal, the
  bow's shortest-life-first pick) exists because DFU restored an
  intent classic shipped broken. Recorded here so nobody "fixes" the
  port back toward classic's bug.
- SPELL REFLECTION REFLECTS THE BUNDLE BUT LANDS THE REST OF IT ANYWAY
  (X11a). `TryReflection` refuses a bundle whose `ReflectedCount > 0`
  (EntityEffectManager.cs:1210-1212), and `IncrementReflectionCount`
  runs INSIDE the per-effect loop of `AssignBundle` (:1228). So on a
  multi-effect spell only the FIRST effect can bounce: the whole bundle
  is reassigned at the caster - who takes ALL of its effects - and
  effects 2..n continue down the target's own loop and land on the
  target as well. A Damage Health + Paralyze spell reflected by its
  target therefore hits the CASTER with both and still paralyses the
  TARGET. It reads like a bug and may be one, but it is what the source
  does and there is no comment claiming otherwise, so it is ported
  verbatim rather than "fixed" toward a guess. Pinned by name in
  `test/x11.test.js` so nobody tidies it away by accident. (The refusal
  itself is deliberate and DFU says so: its own comment offers to raise
  the limit later for "limited reflect volleys", which is exactly why
  the port does not raise it now.)

| USING A DRUG DOES NOTHING (U25). DaggerfallInventoryWindow's drug arm is `InflictPoison(player, player, (Poisons)item.TemplateIndex + 66, true)` under DFU's own comment "Drug poison IDs are 136 through 139. Template indexes are 78 through 81, so add to that" - which needs 58, not 66. 78 + 66 = 144, outside the Poisons enum, so GetClassicPoisonEffectKey formats "Poison-144", no effect is registered under that key and AssignBundle instantiates nothing. The drug is consumed and nothing happens, silently. Ported verbatim and pinned so it cannot be quietly "fixed"; startPoison refuses an unregistered type, which is the same outcome by the same reasoning rather than a guard bolted on | DaggerfallInventoryWindow.UseItem :1766-1770 | Preserved (Ledger B) |

SWEPT 2026-08-19, RE-SWEPT 2026-08-24 (L1). The re-sweep judged all 60
unstruck rows against the tree - ten readers over six rows each, every
STALE verdict then put to a skeptic whose brief was to REFUTE it,
because striking a row that is still real work is the more damaging
error. The skeptic pushed three back to OPEN (the politeness idle, the
PlaySong action, the ResetBonusPool control), and every surviving
verdict was re-checked by hand against its cited lines before the row
was touched. (PlaySong was struck for real on 2026-08-25 - the skeptic
kept a row whose REASON was already false, which is the other half of
this preamble's warning: a row can survive a refutation on its subject
and still be wrong about why.)

TWENTY rows had drifted. Four were struck outright (TalkToStaticNPC,
the enemy loot tail, the title screen, the talk window's four pages);
the rest were narrowed, several with their NUMBERS wrong rather than
their substance: "seventeen unbuilt guild service windows" is two,
"thirty unported effects" is six, "the only trace of banking is
goldPieceWeightInKg" is a 419-line module. Two of the twenty drifted
because of the same session that swept them, which is the failure this
preamble warns about.

The 2026-08-19 sweep's own note, still true: every unstruck row
re-verified against its arc doc and against `src/`. A row here is a CLAIM that something is unported, and a
stale one is worse than a missing one - it sends the next slice to build
what already ships. Four were wrong: breath/drowning + crouch (P12
shipped it, never struck), transition/activation sounds (closed as
verbatim N/A in Audio.md 2026-08-17, never struck here), interior
containers (S2b + E2 shipped two thirds of that row), and the U20b
career flags (S23 and S24 moved five picks from INERT to LIVE). The
rest were confirmed genuinely open, including the four this ledger is
most often read for: lockpicking's ATTEMPT (SHIPPED at R1 2026-08-23 -
both arms, see the struck row below), smaller-dungeon generation, the
interior people gates, and AddSpawnPoints. Re-sweep whenever a slice
closes something routed here.

**LR1 RE-SWEEP (2026-08-29): THREE OF THOSE FOUR ARE NO LONGER OPEN,
and the sentence above outlived them.** Lockpicking was already struck
in its own line when it was written; the interior people gates SHIPPED
at P1 2026-08-25 (`characters/interiorPeople.js`, struck in its row);
AddSpawnPoints CLOSED at HC1 2026-08-28 (struck in its row); and
smaller-dungeon generation is RESERVED by Mac 2026-08-28 to the
enhanced lane, which is not the same thing as open. The paragraph is
kept rather than rewritten because it is a dated record of what that
sweep found - but a reader reaching for "the four" today finds none of
them takeable, which is exactly the rot this preamble warns about,
arriving in the preamble itself.

### Derived figures (GATED - `test/ledger.test.js`)

Four of the twenty rows the 2026-08-24 re-sweep corrected were wrong in
their NUMBERS rather than their substance - "seventeen guild service
windows" when the answer was two, "thirty unported effects" when it was
six. Prose cannot notice that; a reader plans a very different week off
the wrong figure. Every number below is DERIVABLE from a module, so it
is stated once here and pinned, and the pin fails if either side moves
without the other. Rows above may quote these figures but must not
restate them independently.
