| ~~VAMPIRISM / LYCANTHROPY, and the one number it already owes (U24)~~ CORRECTED AND CLOSED (V1 2026-08-24). This row said the temple's cure-disease count is short by one for a player who is turning, and that the arc "lands by filling one parameter". It does not, and the row was wrong in both halves. GetDiseaseCount (EntityEffectManager.cs:1489-1499) counts every bundle of type Disease, and a DFU-NATIVE infection IS one - the DiseaseData is all zeroes at 0xFF days precisely so the disease machinery carries it - so it was ALREADY counted the moment it existed, and V1's pins drive that both ways (an infection counts beside an ordinary disease; EndDisease is the turn's last line, so a cure a minute before the turn works and a minute after has nothing left to cure). PlayerEntity.TimeToBecomeVampireOrWerebeast is read from a CLASSIC .SAV character record (CharacterRecord.cs:242) and is set by nothing else in DFU: its `+1` prices an IMPORTED character who carries the timer without carrying the effect. This port has no classic-save reader, so cureDiseaseOffer's parameter stays false and the count is correct THROUGH THE DISEASE IT ALREADY IS. The parameter is kept, not deleted - a save importer would need it | PlayerEntity.TimeToBecomeVampireOrWerebeast | V1 |
| ~~MacroHelper's TABLE - THREE separate expansion paths~~ **THE WALK UNIFIED (MH1 2026-08-28)**. The third, lawless path is GONE: `questMacros.expandMacroValues` is the ONE walk for window text - maximal-munch `%\w+` with EXACT symbol lookup in the caller's value map (its MCP: a null value leaves the token verbatim, the legacy if-guards as a law), falling to the CONTEXT HANDLERS when a questLike context (machine.macroContext()) rides in, else verbatim (these windows never printed DFU's [undefined] shape). talkSession's greeting/answer sets, guildServiceActions' eight service symbols and arrestFlow's court records all ride it now - the replaceAll chains are deleted, and the walk's exact-match RETIRES their latent corruption (replaceAll('%a') fired inside %adj/%arm). By the census that prompted this slice the leak had grown to 46 replaceAlls across 7 files against the table's 127 handlers; what deliberately REMAINS is not leakage: itemInfo/useItem are the ITEM MCP surface (IM1's recorded design over DFU's item template tokens), buildingNames is BuildingNames.cs's OWN expander (not MacroHelper's table), and townTalk's three are the talk pipeline's lazy SIDE-EFFECT slots (%hnt rolls the reveal, %loc marks the map - values that cannot be precomputed into a map without firing the effect). The two REAL tables stand as they were: questMacros' HANDLERS (GetValue's error-shape ladder, the context table) and talkMacros' MCP (ExpandRandomTextRecord's talk source). A new window now passes a value map to the one walk instead of growing a chain - and can hand machine.macroContext() to get the whole context table for free. 6 mutations, 6 killed. **AND NOW THERE IS ONE TABLE (E-group E7, 2026-09-02)** - the row's own ask, "the table is one file in DFU and wants one slice here", finished. questMacros' HANDLERS carries all 217 rows of `macroHandlers` (M-X's ELSEWHERE map of thirty-seven rows recorded at their consuming windows is gone: the item/painting/service families as ItemName-style call-throughs, the talk MCP's %hnt/%hnt2/%pqn/%pqp, the talk GLOBALS %1com/%key/%loc/%fcn/%hnr, %gii/%map and the court four, and the 217th row - the bare `%`, which prints a percent sign and which the port had been DELETING out of talk text). `systems/talkMacros.js` carries NO table at all now: it is the MCP C# supplies - TalkManagerDataSource's thirteen overrides plus the GameManager the global rows read (the host hands the quest machine's macro hooks straight in, one GameManager as DFU has one) - and the shared ladder is exported as `getMacroValue`. Two shipped corrections fell out of the consolidation: %lev and %pct are ONE C# row (`{"%lev", GuildTitle}`), so both fall through GuildTitle's mcp-null arm to the player's name where %lev used to answer a sentinel; and Date (%dat) is a GLOBAL off WorldTime, not the quest's own clock, so it answers in every context. 7 mutations, 7 killed. Original text (U24; NARROWED). `systems/quest/questMacros.js` ports GetValue's error-shape ladder plus ~40 handlers for the quest path (Q4-i) and `systems/talkMacros.js` ports ExpandRandomTextRecord's talk MCP, so two REAL handler tables now exist where the row assumed none. The ad-hoc string path the row describes is still there and has GROWN: `systems/talkSession.js:76-83` and `:99-103` plus `systems/guildServiceActions.js:48-67` replaceAll fourteen macros between them (the row says eleven), including %dwr and %cpn which it does not name. So the count is wrong in both directions - more engine than the row credits, more leakage than it admits. Original text: 100+ macros; the port expands eleven - %pcn/%pcf/%cn/%oth/%hnt/%key/%hnr/%ra in talkSession.js and %a/%gii/%god/%pct in guildServiceActions.js. Each new window discovers the two or three it needs, which is a slow leak rather than a port: the table is one file in DFU and wants one slice here | Utility/MacroHelper.cs | UI arc |

| ~~GOLD IS A BAG STACK WHERE CLASSIC KEEPS A COUNTER (`src/systems/inventory.js:48`)~~ **SHIPPED (E-group, 2026-09-02): gold is `PlayerEntity.GoldPieces` now, and `PlayerEntity.Items` can never hold Currency.** The seam is DFU's own: `DoTransferItem` (`DaggerfallInventoryWindow.cs:1562-1571`) intercepts a `Currency.Gold_pieces` item whose destination is `PlayerEntity.Items`, does `GoldPieces += item.stackCount`, `from.RemoveItem(item)`, plays `SoundClips.GoldPieces` and RETURNS - so the pile is spent into the counter at the door and no later code has to exclude it. `systems/itemTransfer.js`'s `applyTransfer` carries that arm for every screen that transfers (`toPlayer` is DFU's `PlayerEntity.Items == to`), and it ANSWERS NULL, because the `return` also skips `EquipItem` (:1580) and the choose-one close (:1585-1591) - so a reward pile of gold is taken without closing the picker, DFU's quirk and not the port's. Both doors run it: `ui/nativeInventory.js:_pickRemote` and `ui/enhancedInventory.js:take`. Every other producer writes the counter as DFU's does - `startingGear` (`ItemHelper.cs:1354`), biography's GP command (`BiogFile.cs:283-289`, its recorded minus-arm departure intact), the pickpocket (`PlayerActivate.cs:1628`), `classicSave`'s physicalGold (`StartGameBehaviour.cs:603`), the quest machine's `GetItem` gold arm (`GetItem.cs:73-78`) - and the four readers go through `court.js` (`goldAmount`/`addGold`/`deductGold`/`deductGoldPieces`), which is now `PlayerEntity.GetGoldAmount`/`DeductGoldAmount` on the counter rather than on a stack. WEIGHT came back as DFU writes it: `inventory.carriedWeight` IS `PlayerEntity.CarriedWeight` (`:184`), `Items.GetWeight() + goldPieces * goldPieceWeightInKg`, and its four readers are DFU's four - the sheet's encumbrance line, `GetCarriedWeight()` inside `CanCarryAmount`, `DaggerfallBankManager.cs:370`'s withdrawal test and `LevitateMotor.cs:83`'s over-encumbered walk (all three motor hosts feed it). The wagon KEEPS its stack, because DFU's does (`DaggerfallBankManager.cs:343` reads it back), and so does every loot pile and quest gold resource - `ItemBuilder.CreateGoldPieces`, `value = 1` and all. SAVE: `snap.goldPieces` beside the collections (`SerializablePlayer.cs:133`/`:302`); a PRE-E4 save's Currency stack is absorbed into the counter on restore, BELOW the index-keyed `lightSourceIndex` relink (removing a row above it would light the wrong item) and ONLY when the envelope carries no counter - because `GivePc.cs:179/:186` can legitimately put a Currency stack in `PlayerEntity.Items` in DFU too, unspendable there as here. Pinned across `audit18_systems_items` (the door, both directions), `littlelaws` (the carry gate over `CarriedWeight`, the split-take into the counter), `nativeinventory`/`wagon` (drop-gold reads and writes the counter), `save` (the envelope, the migration's order and its condition), `audit17f` (the producers and the invariant), `audit26_movement` (the member and all three motor hosts) | DaggerfallInventoryWindow.DoTransferItem (:1562-1571); PlayerEntity.cs :64/:158/:184/:1313-1354 | Systems arc |

| FaceUVTool's 1,803-UV residual at matched precision **NARROWED (E-group, 2026-09-02) - the surface is fenced and the harness is reproducible; the number itself still needs ARENA2.** Three things are now RULED OUT by reading DFU rather than by measuring it. (a) `API/Vector3.cs` is DOUBLE throughout - x/y/z (:35-45), Magnitude (:151-160), DotProduct (:508-520) and Normalize's `double inverse = 1 / Magnitude` (:583-597) - so P0..P2, V0, V1, the orthogonalisation, both magnitudes, both normalizes and all six basis dot products were ALREADY matched on both sides and cannot carry this. (b) Points 0, 1 and 2 of every face are pure Int32 delta sums (FaceUVTool.cs:172-186) with no floating point on the path, so the whole residual lives at point index >= 3 - only on faces with MORE THAN THREE corners. (c) `Math.trunc` and `(Int32)` agree on every in-range value, negatives included. What is RULED IN is the one genuinely single-precision half of the routine: `df3duvparams_lt` (float[] X/Y/Z/U/V, :55-63), `df3duvmatrix_t` (float UA..VD, :66-76) and `l_ComputeDFUVMatrixXY`'s `float determinant` + `float[] Xi/Yi/Zi` (:222-229), ending in `(Int32)((pn.x * UA) + (pn.y * UB) + UD)` - a float expression truncated. `test/faceuvresidue.test.js` measures that seam without ARENA2: over 400,000 random Daggerfall-scale four-point faces, 76 (0.019%) land on opposite sides of the final truncation under the two precisions, and one of them is pinned (point 3's v is 11009 in the port's doubles, 11010 under DFU's declared floats). The flip needs BOTH halves at once - rounding only the matrix coefficients, or only the final accumulate, leaves that face at 11009 - which is why the pin's mutation is the whole chain and a half-mutation proves nothing. AND THE MEASUREMENT WAS NOT REPRODUCIBLE: the float->double widening this row credits was never one of the five committed harness patches, so nobody with ARENA2 and this repo could re-run it. `tools/parity/patches/FaceUVTool.cs.patch` is that edit, wired into `tools/parity/prepare.sh` and marked as the one patch that is NOT behaviour-neutral. Re-running is `bash tools/parity/prepare.sh` then `ARENA2_PATH=... bash tools/parity/run.sh`; the faceuv line's second column is the number. | Readers arc |

TK-iii SHIPPED (2026-08-21, THE ANSWER PIPELINE: systems/answerPipeline.js is TalkManager's question/answer half 1:1 over T3's tables - the question ladder, the answer dispatch, the tell-me-about/where-is arms, GetAnswerWhereAmI's four arms, GetNPCKnowledgeAboutItem's ladder, the regional building walk with its THREE-FLAG-BYTE key decoder (truncate-before-shift reads bit 7-n), the hint fork, GetDialogHint/2, the compass and map marks; kept quirks by line incl. THE KNOWS-BUT-SILENT ARM and the asymmetric where-is gate; 40 pins). AUDIT III same day: the raw-C# main-loop re-read found THREE port bugs, all one mistake - state whose only observer runs somewhere the port had stopped thinking about, each behind a comment claiming faithfulness: the tone gate (C# recomputes the reaction tier INSIDE GetAnswerText :1994-1995, not in the caller), THE %n SLOT (greetingNameNPC is filled before the greeting record expands and emptied the instant it returns :1136-1140, because TalkManagerMCP's Name() resolves %n through it; the port stored it after and never cleared it), and THE HEADLESS SESSION (npcData is a FIELD :189 whose one-answer counter survives from answer to answer; the port's absent-seam default was an object literal rebuilt per call, so the gate could never close - the rumor mill carried the same bug, and AUDIT I had recorded its symptom as a proven equivalence, now withdrawn). Added the localizedLocationName seam GetRegionalLocationCityName reads (:1885). Four-round campaign: round 3 swept 170 mutants over the whole file against a freshly regenerated coverage map - 147 kills, 23 survivors, 0 uncovered lines (round 2 on a stale map had reported 26); 11 real gaps pinned and re-confirmed dead against the full suite at fails=5 on a baseline of 4, 12 proven equivalents. TK-iv SHIPPED (2026-08-21, THE NPC SESSION: systems/npcSession.js is TalkManager's NPC-session half 1:1 - the click arms, the target chain, GetStaticNPCFactionData with the id-0 split and the three Random_* redirects, both parent walks, GetGreetingIndex whole, GetNPCGreetingRecord, TalkToNpc's three doors, GetNPCQuestGreeting, the questor pool and its tracking family, StartNewConversation, and the npcsWithWork/castleNPCsSpokenTo save halves; kept quirks by line: THE UNGUARDED FIRST ARM (&& binds tighter, so the ally/enemy guard covers only the reversed test), THE STRANGER FLOOR (index 8 at rep 6-29 matches neither arm and falls through), THE REJECTION FOUND MID-GREETING (the record lookup sets alreadyRejectedOnce and the second test answers with raw tokens in a box), THE NAMES NOBODY READS (four faction-name fields written by every arm and read by nothing in the DFU tree), the sgroup clamp at 5, the repeat-click no-op, the questor roll spent before the child and in-pool tests; recorded not emulated: THE ZERO-FACTION MATCH, where an unresolvable guild reads as faction 0 and every unset relation slot matches it; the parity re-read caught a bug in the port's own StartNewConversation, which reset three fields that live on TK-iii's pipeline - now routed through a seam, with the pipeline gaining the matching half; 69 pins + 1 on the pipeline + 1 on the tree). AUDIT IV same day: the raw-C# main-loop re-read found THREE port bugs, TWO of them the mistake TK-iii's re-read had already caught once - a law left with the host that C# keeps inside the method (half of TalkToNpc's tone reset; the shadowed rebuildTopicLists) - plus the questor pool taking each candidate's social group as given where C# resolves it through GetStaticNPCFactionData with the building's own type, unclamped; kept with them, DFU's own THE UNCLONED QUESTOR POST (the stored post-quest message is permanently expanded by the first greeting, where GetAnswerFromTokensArray clones for exactly that reason). The mount also exposed a TK-ii gap: ResetNPCKnowledge (:546-553), which walks all four lists AND asks for a rebuild. Three-round campaign: 180 mutants then 190 at a fresh seed, 0 uncovered lines both times, kill rate 68% then 87%, 16 real gaps pinned and re-confirmed dead against the full suite, and 8 proven equivalents in two families - the in-common loops' bounds (a fourth read is undefined, and no faction record carries a non-numeric slot) and DFU's own redundant `sameTalkTargetAsBefore = false` at the head of both click arms, dead because SetTargetNPC assigns the same field by ref as its own first line; THE BASELINE TRAP sprung again on those two and caught, since they came back at exactly the baseline. THE LESSON recorded as arc law: a guard is invisible on the index that does not reach it - every `> N` in GetGreetingIndex differs only when the index is EXACTLY N, which no single membership can arrange, so nine thoroughly-pinned-looking guards were untested until six two-membership fixtures walked the index onto each bar. TK-v SHIPPING (2026-08-21, THE HOST MOUNT: systems/talkMacros.js is TalkManagerMCP.cs whole - 13 handlers - plus MacroHelper.ExpandMacros and ExpandRandomTextRecord, which is the reader that finally makes TK-iii's two live slots observable (%n's greeting name and %loc's map-reveal flag, both pinned from inside an actual expansion); with them THE MALE-NAME SEED NUDGE, %oth's faction-race fix, the four questor pronouns and %hnt/%hnt2 parting only on the quest arm. The re-read caught the expansion ALGORITHM: MacroHelper scans from each % to the next terminator rather than replacing substrings, which brought THE MACRO CACHE (one dictionary per call, so a record naming %fn twice names the same woman twice) and THE PIPE IS EATEN (%di|ern -> southern). Host wiring: the session and pipeline built beside the mill and the tree and cross-wired as TalkManager's fields are; THE QUESTOR DOOR OPEN, so a static-NPC click carrying work opens Q4-ii's offerSocialQuest - the arm waiting since the offer flow shipped; the tone gate, the reaction cache and the question counter moved OFF townTalk onto the engine; SaveDataConversation whole in the quicksave; the six event subscriptions wired. Supporting: OATH_RACE_INDEX widened to the eight GetFactionRaceFromRace answers, and dfRandom given the Seed accessor pair C#'s settable property is. test/talkengine.test.js builds all four modules as world.js does and drives a conversation through the assembly - all six pins passed on the first run. 14 + 6 pins.) | **THE DAGGERFALL -> MORROWIND WEAPON MAPPING** (`src/formats/mwFirstPerson.js`, `DF_TO_MW_WEAPON`, MW-D9 2026-08-29). Daggerfall's weapon taxonomy is not Morrowind's, and the rules doc had already ruled on this shape before any code existed: any mapping between them "is a PORT DECISION, not a ported rule, and belongs in the recorded divergences with its reasoning visible - not inferred inside a lookup table, where the last attempt hid it wrong twice." So the table is EXPORTED, pinned row by row, and keyed by the port's own WEAPONS **template index** rather than by the sprite layer's `WEAPON_TYPES` - which folds a Claymore and a Longsword into one class and therefore cannot tell a one-hander from a two-hander. Eighteen rows, one per wieldable template (Arrow is ammunition and is the only omission). MOST ROWS ARE NEAR-TRANSLATIONS: the short blades, the long blades, the axes (Daggerfall's War Axe / Battle Axe split maps cleanly onto AxeOneHand / AxeTwoHand), and both bows onto MarksmanBow. **FOUR ROWS ARE JUDGEMENT AND ARE MAC'S TO OVERRULE:** (1) **FLAIL** - Morrowind has no flail at all; BluntOneHand is the nearest thing that exists and nothing here is right. (2) **STAFF** -> BluntTwoWide, matching where Morrowind's own staves sit. (3) **CLAYMORE and DAI-KATANA** -> LongBladeTwoHand, which is a BEHAVIOURAL change rather than a mesh swap: it costs them Daggerfall's own one-handed animation. (4) **MATERIAL** - `DF_TO_MW_MATERIAL` folds Daggerfall's ten materials onto the seven Morrowind ships, so Mithril and Elven both read as glass, and Adamantium / Orcish / Ebony all read as ebony. A type the player's archives do not carry draws NOTHING and says so on the card, because a longsword standing in for a bow would hang on the wrong bone, in the wrong hand, and look entirely deliberate | weapontype.cpp's eleven short groups vs `WEAPONS` (18 templates) | DECLARED MW-D9 2026-08-29, awaiting Mac's read - the four judgement rows are the ones to argue with |
| **THE UNCHARGED DAGGERFALL SWING** (`src/combat/fpArm.js`, MW-D12 2026-08-30). Morrowind's blow is CHARGED: the wind-up section plays while the button is held, and `calculateWindUp` reads how far into the `min attack` -> `max attack` window the clip got when it was released. That fraction picks how much of the release is skipped and which of the small/medium/large follow keys the blow ends on. **Daggerfall has no charge at all** - the swing fires the instant the drag crosses the gesture threshold and its damage does not vary with how long you held the button. So the port releases the wind-up the moment its section ENDS, which puts the playhead exactly on `max attack` and makes calculateWindUp answer 1: a Daggerfall swing is a Morrowind swing released at full charge. That is the reference's own arithmetic on the port's own clock, not a constant somebody chose, and it is why every blow takes the LARGE follow keys. THE ONE PLACE A HOLD WOULD BE RIGHT is the bow, and Morrowind's draw-and-hold is wired to the port's own machine (`isBow && state === 'StrikeUp'`) rather than to "is it a bow": the in-game bow path is DFU's BowDrawback-OFF instant shot (`playerWeapon.gesture`), so today the arm never holds, and coupling the hold to the weapon TYPE would have parked it at full draw waiting for a release the game path never sends. It goes live the moment DFU's drawback arm does, with nothing in the arm to change | `CharacterController::calculateWindUp` + `prepareHit` (character.cpp:1235-1259) vs `PlayerWeapon.gesture` | DECLARED MW-D12 2026-08-30 - the note to argue with is whether a Daggerfall swing should read as a FULL-strength Morrowind blow or a random one (the reference's no-window fallback is `min(1, 0.1 + rnd)`) |

| **THE SETTINGS SCREEN'S PLAYER-FACING TAXONOMY** (`src/ui/settingsMap.js`, MENU-slice). The store's 13 ini sections are where DFU WRITES a value; they are not how a person looks for one - two of the three settings a casual player most wants (Sound Volume, Music Volume) live in `[Controls]`, and Field Of View sits in `[Video]` beside twenty-four keys about shadow-map resolution. So the screen carries its own 7-category map over the same 171 keys. It stays close to DFU's own UI grouping (`DaggerfallAdvancedSettingsWindow.cs:234-238`: gamePlay / interface / enhancements / video / accessibility) with TWO DELIBERATE DEPARTURES: **AUDIO is its own category** where DFU buries it as a section on the gamePlay page (`:264`), because volume is the most-reached-for setting in any game; and **MODS is its own category** rather than a section under enhancements (`:327`), so the mod manager has a home to grow into rather than a corner to be added to. The map is TOTAL and DISJOINT over all 171 keys and pinned both ways, so a DFU re-bake that adds or renames a key fails the build instead of dropping a row off the screen | DaggerfallAdvancedSettingsWindow's page/section split | Approved 2026-08-21 (Mac: "game settings need a home, audio needs a home, graphics needs a home, the future mod manager needs a home") |

The keyed art-less path survives as the fallback. **c2/S6 2026-09-01 then landed the shader's SECOND HALF**: the six presentations (below-slice colour/grayscale unchanged, above-slice TRANSPARENT at alpha 0.75, above-slice WIREFRAME at (0.9,0.9,0.7,0.6) / (0.25,0.25,0.25,0.6)) with Cutout as the ABSENCE of the above-slice group, the inverted clip that makes the two passes a partition, the `min(y, slice)` that leaves the above-slice dim IDENTICALLY ZERO (DFU never dims it - a port that does is wrong), the water tint both passes carry fed per block from S1's `automapWaterLevel` - whose COLOUR is `UnderwaterFog.waterMapColor` (0.1,0.3,0.25,0.4), the value Automap.cs:2589-2590 injects into the ONE automap material before AutomapModel.cs:83 Instantiates it onto every submesh, NOT the `.shader` Properties default (0,0.3,0.5,0.4) DFU overwrites before a fragment is ever shaded; HideAll turning MeshRenderer.enabled off WITHOUT touching the grayscale keyword is what forced `revealed` to become the draw gate and the tier a choice inside it. One recorded departure, rowed above (the key's yaw-built quaternion); one live defect fixed on the way (`ui/automapChrome.js` threw on any MIDDLE-button release - `held` has no middle set - which is the slice drag's own release). **c2/S9 2026-09-01 then landed the INTERIOR-BUILDING ARM**, with DFU's own quirks reproduced and recorded at the site so a later reviewer cannot fix them out of parity: `interiorContext` mints per-model keys (`int:<placement index>`, outside the dungeon's `${bi}:${position}` space by construction) and world AABBs at the ONE push site every building entry runs, builds the reveal model over them and runs the same 5 Hz probe set indoors (CheckForNewlyDiscoveredMeshes covers IsPlayerInsideBuilding at :1155); M opens the window inside a building in BOTH interior hosts, with all three pointer phases routed to the interior overlay slot so the map's press-hold buttons and drags work there. The interior differences are DFU's: geometry is ALWAYS presented in COLOUR (AutomapModel.cs:46-72 passes `visitedInThisEntering = playerIsInsideBuilding`, so RENDER_IN_GRAYSCALE is disabled on every interior material and never enabled again - a LAW in `_partitionDraws`, not an accident of the two sets agreeing); SetupBeacons places the entrance beacon at the entered door and sets it DISCOVERED (:1450-1457) and RestoreStateAutomapDungeon(true)'s opening HideAll() takes it straight back out (:2355, :2460), so a building's map opens FULLY HIDDEN with an undiscovered entrance that re-lights on the next LOS tick - pinned as a SEQUENCE, including the tail at :2379 where a dungeon record sharing the town's "RegionName/Name" lights the shop's beacon anyway; interior discovery is PER-VISIT (the locationName mismatch at :2381 ends the restore); there is NO micro-map indoors (:1722-1748's null arm); the reset default is CUTOUT; and S8's note/teleporter click arm stays gated off. `RestoreStateAutomapInterior` is DEAD CODE in the reference - written, and called from nowhere - and is deliberately not ported. THE ARC'S WHOLE RENDERER SURFACE, stated in full because an earlier draft of this clause claimed "one new GL call": c2/S6 added `setAutomapWater`, `drawMeshWire` with its private `_ensureWireMesh` and the exported pure `buildWireIndices` - the arc's ONLY new GL primitive, `gl.LINES` over an edge index buffer - and c2/S10 HALF A added `drawScreenQuad`'s optional `rotate` plus the renderer-owned `screenScissor` bracket, which introduces no new GL call (setScreenScissor/clearScreenScissor predate the arc). S5, S7, S8, S9 and HALF B touched renderer.js not at all. HALF B - THE PLATE ANCHOR MOVED and EVERY PLATE IN EVERY TOWN MOVED WITH IT: the anchor is now the building subrecord's own Position (ExteriorAutomap.cs:664-665) over ALL buildings of ALL blocks via the new `world/buildingSummaries.js`, retiring the exterior-DOOR substitution; plus `customUserDisplayName` on the discovery record with the double-click rename (drawn in the label's place while the tooltip keeps the canonical name) and revealUndiscoveredBuildings. THE CORNERS STAY AXIS-ALIGNED: RotateBuildingNameplates' corner rotation is DEAD CODE in DFU (UpdateAutomapView recomputes all four corners axis-aligned before ComputeNameplateOffsets reads them) and porting it would itself be the departure. **ROAD-C c2 flight-2 REVIEW then corrected ten things in that window and rowed them here** (2026-09-02): the ROTATION SIGN (`ui/automapCamera.js`'s two exterior rotate laws moved the yaw the WRONG WAY - `AngleAxis(A, -up) == AngleAxis(-A, +up)`, so ActionRotate's `RotateAround(pos, -up, -amount*dt)` RAISES the camera's euler-y, which is exactly the basis `toPanelScreen`/`exteriorDragPan` are written in; rotate-around-player also turned its centre one way and its basis the other, so the marker it pivots on swung across the panel instead of staying pinned); THE HOTKEYS ARE NOW DFU'S OWN - the window reads all thirty `ExtAutomap*` rows of `systems/dialogShortcuts.js` (DialogShortcuts.txt:267-296) through the same ShortcutOrFallback door `ui/automapWindow.js` reads the `Automap*` half with, in DFU's two poll classes, replacing an invented WASD/QE/digit table that matched classic at no key at all; with it come the three DIRECT view-mode verbs (F2/F3/F4, :1235-1262) that had no arm anywhere, the two rotate-around-player rows that were mouse-only, keyboard zoom-OUT (A2's `case 'minus'` could never fire - ui/input.js returns 'char:-' for the hyphen), and the two-phase `isCloseWindowDeferred` toggle-close; the two panel DRAGS now scale their native delta back to REAL SCREEN pixels, which is the space `InputManager.MousePosition` and DFU's two speed constants live in (the dungeon window already read the identical idiom that way), and the right drag carries `ActionRotate`'s own `Time.unscaledDeltaTime` instead of folding it in as 1; the hover seam's (-1,-1) SENTINEL no longer reaches the chrome's drag machine (a fabricated coordinate, not a position - it teleported the map ~165 world units on any pointer excursion off the letterboxed panel); the chrome's `sound` output reaches `audio.playOneShot(SOUND.ButtonClick)`, which is what makes `ActionClickSoundOnly` mean anything; and the camera ROTATION is remembered across close/reopen beside the zoom, as `ExteriorAutomap.cs:88`'s `cameraTransformRotationSaved` is. The same review's second lane corrected the PLATES AND CHROME: the double-click rename prompt now opens on the name the player is LOOKING AT, because `mb.TextBox.Text = renamingLabelRef.Text` (ExteriorAutomap.cs:888) is the label's drawn text - the custom name whenever one is set - while the canonical stays TextBox.Name/DefaultText (:887, :889), the fallback for an emptied box; before this, re-editing a renamed building opened on the canonical and a player who confirmed unchanged silently reverted their own rename; that rename push no longer LATCHES the panel drag - the double click is a press on the render panel, so it arms the pan flag, and its release is routed to townTalk's slot, which by then holds the input box, so the window gained `onReturn` (windowStack's RemoveWindow tells the uncovered window) to release the chrome the way DFU's polled `PanelAutomap_OnMouseUp` does on the first frame after the box pops (BaseScreenComponent.cs:643-648); the compass is now hud.js's ONE `drawCompassStrip` - the dummy panel gives the POSITION alone (:456) and HUDCompass sizes the frame from the ART (HUDCompass.cs:132-135), so it is COMPBOX.IMG's 69x17 with a 65px strip window, not the dummy panel's 76x17 stretched, and the second copy of the scroll/inset arithmetic is gone; and the plate tooltip is toolTip.js's ONE `drawToolTipBox` at the CURSOR (ToolTip.cs:158-179) in the two GUI colour settings DFU assigns it by hand (:874-875), replacing a bespoke black box at the plate's own x - with `ignoreEnableSetting`, the one exception DFU itself carries, because `nameplateToolTip` is a bare `new ToolTip()` (:870-871) drawn unconditionally (:571-572) rather than the window's EnableToolTips-gated `defaultToolTip`. DEPARTURES THAT STAND: the exterior window's HELD keyboard arms are edge-driven - one frame of DFU's per-second speed per press, because townTalk's overlay seam delivers keydown and no keyup, with the browser's key repeat supplying the hold; it covers the IsPressedWith class alone, since DFU's focus/reset/view-mode/background arms are IsDownWith (:599-641) and so are parity here (the MOUSE arms, chrome press-hold and both drags, are frame-exact); the plate label is the port's FNT text at half TextScale rather than DFU's yellow-reloaded DaggerfallFont Texture2D. **ROAD-D D5 (2026-09-02) THEN CLOSED TWO OF THE THREE RESIDUE ITEMS, both because the blocker each was written against had gone stale.** THE RESIDENCE-WITH-ACTIVE-QUEST PLATE ARM: the port DOES expose locationWasMarkedOnMapByNPC - `systems/topicTree.js:394-419` is IsBuildingQuestResource verbatim, flag and override name both, pinned since TK-ii - it simply had no production consumer, so `questName` had no producer anywhere in src/ and the arm could never fire; a quest-marked residence was silently plateless where ExteriorAutomap.cs:705-707 names it. `residenceQuestName` + `stampResidenceQuestNames` (`ui/exteriorAutomapWindow.js`) are now that C#'s own DOUBLE LOOP over it, with its three quirks kept and named at the site - there is NO break, so the LAST marked match wins; the `&&` SHORT-CIRCUITS, so IsBuildingQuestResource is never asked about a place whose buildingKey does not match; and the name that lands is the OUTER place's `SiteDetails.buildingName`, NOT the inner call's `overrideBuildingName`, which parts from it exactly when the key-only outer guard matches a place in another town (buildingKeys are location-relative and really do collide) while the inner mapID+key test matches one here. The stamp happens where DFU's does - once per nameplate build, at window open (:273), never per frame - in the M-outside door of `scenes/world.js`, the host that has a quest machine; `scenes/exterior.js` mounts none, so its every residence takes DFU's own empty-set answer and says so at the site. THE TEN BUTTON TOOLTIPS: Internal_Strings.csv:908-917 now sits beside the dungeon block in `ui/automapText.js` byte for byte - including the two misprinted parentheses (`exteriorAutomapToolTipUpstairsButton` closes one it never opens, `...DownstairsButton` opens one it never closes) and the double space on the rotate-left row - formatted by THE SAME two laws the dungeon half uses (ShortcutOrFallback onto the same KeyCode.Home, String.Format over HotkeySequence.ToString) with the `ExtAutomap*` names, in DFU's own argument order rather than the sentences' (the stair rows take ZoomIn/ZoomOut, not the Upstairs/Downstairs rows their BUTTONS are named after); they are driven by the chrome's rest clock, which `tick()` was already computing and this window was throwing away, and unlike the plate tooltip they DO honour EnableToolTips because they ride the shared `defaultToolTip`. ~~FLAGGED residue, now ONE item, at `src/ui/exteriorAutomapWindow.js`'s header and nowhere else: map_revealbuildings / map_hidebuildings (the flag they set, revealUndiscoveredBuildings, is live and pinned; the port has no console for them, exactly as ui/travelMapWindow.js records for map_reveallocations)~~ **SHIPPED (E-group, 2026-09-02): the port has a console command database now** - `src/systems/consoleCommands.js` is Wenzil.Console's ConsoleCommandsDatabase/ConsoleCommand/HelpCommand/ConsoleInputHistory and ConsoleController's submit law, and all EIGHT registered map commands hang off it: this window's pair (registered from both exterior hosts, its flag moved to module scope where DFU's persistent component keeps it), the dungeon automap's trio (registered from `systems/automap.js` where the laws already were, with `Automap.instance` now latched at bindAutomapLayout and dropped by both exits - the standalone host's `window.__automapCommand` probe runs the database instead of repeating its three answers), and the travel map's trio including `map_reveallocation`, whose PlayerGPS.DiscoverLocation throw is reported and whose short-argument arm answers with HELP's own details block. The departure that had no Ledger row is retired rather than given one).** - ~~six of seven unbuilt~~ THE SPELL MAKER SHIPPED (S1 2026-08-24: `ui/spellMakerWindow.js` + `systems/spellMaker.js` + `systems/spellEffects.js` - the shared dependency, THE EFFECT TEMPLATE REGISTRY, is what spellEffects.js now IS: all 90 rows DFU offers at the SpellMaker station with their group/subgroup names and the three support flags that gate the settings editor's panels. The maker builds a SPELLS.STD-shaped record (the recorded storage substitution for EffectBundleSettings), prices it through the port's existing cost home, runs DFU's purchase ladder in order, and inscribes into the player's book; made spells now survive the save, which needed the envelope to stop storing bare file indexes. Entry is the Mages Guild / Kynareth MakeSpells service. FLAGGED residue: the native INFO01I0 art window, the icon picker (nothing draws spell icons yet), and the 30 catalog rows whose runtime arm the port has not built - offered 1:1 but MARKED inert so no one buys a spell that cannot fire). ~~Still unbuilt: the colour picker and the SpellIconPickerWindow~~ **NOTHING LEFT UNBUILT (CP1 2026-08-28 shipped the colour picker, the row's last window)**: `ui/colorPicker.js` is ColorPicker.cs whole - the 360-hue slider (DisplayUnits 50, TotalUnits 50+360-1, trough clicks paging by DisplayUnits), the (int)-cast 186x90 S/V picture with GetPixel's flipped-y sample, the parse-at-exactly-eight hex box, Unity's RGBToHSV/HSVToRGB laws, and the panel whose OWN background is the live picked colour (ConfirmColorPicked). Its one DFU consumer is the advanced settings screen's colour rows, and that is the port's consumer too: the seven live-tier colour settings (tooltip pair, four automap building colours, two micro-map colours) were read-only detail dialogs until now - a colour row's change gesture opens the picker seeded from the live RRGGBBAA value, OK commits the eight digits, Escape (or a tap outside, the house decline posture) keeps the old value. ALPHA RIDES THE HEX BOX ALONE, DFU's own quirk kept: picking by eye discards a translucent setting's alpha. Recorded departures: the crosshair drag collapses to tap-to-place (the settings host's single-shot law), GetPixel's one out-of-range edge clamps where Unity's Repeat wrap reads black, Slider_OnScroll's half-crosshair sample offset (an art-size artifact of a PNG the port does not carry) is recorded not reproduced, and the panel clamps to the elastic page keeping DFU's (int) proportions. 13 mutations, 13 killed - the negative-hue wrap needed its pin moved onto the RETURNED h, because hsvToRgb's own wrap hid its absence from every round-trip. (**MC1 2026-08-28 shipped the icon picker** - `ui/spellIconPickerWindow.js`, wired into BOTH DFU consumers: the spellbook's icon panel and the keyed spell maker, whose icon row's ENTER pushes the picker and whose +/- is the Next/PreviousIconButton wrap cycle over the classic 69; the picker is the one pointer-driven surface over that keyed sheet. It closed TWO DEAD SEAMS on the way: GUI/LargeHUD, written by the pause window since I3 and read by nothing, and Actions.ActivateCursor, bound to Enter since I1 with no consumer at all - which is PlayerMouseLook.cursorActive, the flag IsLargeHUDInteractable IS, so without it the panels would have been unreachable). ~~HUDActiveSpells~~ SHIPPED TOO (U46 2026-08-25: ui/hudActiveSpells.js - the eight layout schemes, the shared 24-slot pool, the caster split, the `< 2` expiry and its 4Hz blink that an ITEM never joins, the large-HUD lift that only goes upward, and the tooltip. Two more fields joined the bundle stamp - the spell's icon and whether the player cast it - because neither is derivable once the entries are on the list. And the icon SHEET moved to load with the HUD art: it had loaded only with the spellbook window, so the rows were invisible until the player happened to open their book once. GUI/IconsPositioningScheme is tiered live at last). ~~HUDEscortingNPCFaces~~ SHIPPED (FE1 2026-08-28: `src/ui/hudEscortFaces.js` - EscortingNPCFacePanel whole: CreateFaceDetails verbatim with the Individual's FACTION.TXT face read, the Children-514 variant, the always-Breton foe roll (both ENGINE-PRNG injectable), the 60/61/502 resolution bands into FACES.CIF / TFAC00I0.RCI / the race heads / KIDS00I0.CIF, the (8,36) column layout with maxFaces 3, the quest-end sweep on a NEW machine onQuestEnded seam, and SaveData_v1.escortingFaces riding composeSessionState. ONE RECORDED DEPARTURE: DFU loads face textures synchronously inside RefreshFaces and THROWS on a missing one; the port's art is async and data-gated like the rest of the HUD - a face draws from the frame its art lands, a missing record costs that face (warned once), never the session. QUIRK KEPT: DaggerfallHUD.cs:207 wires ShowEscortingFaces to EnableBorder, a no-op, so the faces are unconditional). ~~the two large-HUD settings still read by nothing - LargeHUDOffsetHorse and LargeHUDUndockedOffsetWeapon~~ **BOTH LIVE at ROAD-D D10**: `ui/hudLarge.js` is the one home for `horseOffsetHeight` (TransportManager.cs:304-309 - the drawn bar's height when LargeHUD and LargeHUDOffsetHorse are on, and it never asks about docking) and `weaponOffsetHeight` (FPSWeapon.cs:146-155 - the same height, but a DOCKED bar FORCES it whatever the setting says, DFU's own anti-misconfiguration arm); `scenes/world.js` feeds the first into `ridingRect` and `combat/fpsWeapon.js` takes the second as its default `offsetHeight`, so the viewmodel is no longer drawn underneath a docked bar. Both keys are tiered live. ~~AND THE DOCKED BAR STILL OCCLUDED THE WORLD INSTEAD OF SHRINKING IT (AUDIT 39 F135, flagged at `ui/hudLarge.js:75`)~~ **SHIPPED (ROAD-E E5, 2026-09-02)**: `Utility/ViewportChanger.cs:52-67` and `HUDCrosshair.cs:43-52` are both live and they had to land together. `ui/hudLarge.js` is the one home for all three terms - `dockedLargeHudHeight` (`LargeHUD.ScreenHeight` gated on LargeHUD AND LargeHUDDocked, which is NOT the horse arm's gate), `largeHudViewportRect` (DFU's `new Rect(0, hudHeight, 1, 1 - hudHeight)` digit for digit; Unity's camera rect is normalized bottom-left, which is `gl.viewport`'s own space, so it crosses over without a flip) and `largeHudWorldAspect` (Unity derives a camera's aspect from its viewport, so a rect shrunk without its denominator would STRETCH the world into the strip rather than crop the lens). The rect is RENDERER-OWNED FRAME STATE - `Renderer.setWorldViewport` is consumed by the next `beginFrame` exactly as ViewportChanger recomputes it every frame, so no menu, video or map scene can inherit a world frame's strip; the 2D passes take the whole canvas back at the first `drawScreenQuad`, the port's one screen-space primitive, so no host has to remember it; and both the character-sprite RT (which runs mid-world-pass) and the panel bracket now BORROW AND RETURN the rect, the EV6 shape the clear colour has had since F034. All four hosts that draw the bar carry it - `scenes/world.js`, `scenes/exterior.js`, `scenes/worldModes.js` (both modal arms) and `scenes/dungeon.js` - with the sky and EV8's far province ring sharing the lens; `scenes/interior.js` draws no HUD at all, so there is no surface there. The crosshair re-centres through `ui/hudCrosshair.js`'s `crosshairCentreY`: DFU's `(Screen.height - ScreenHeight - crosshairSize.y) / 2` is the reticle's TOP, so its CENTRE is `(Screen.height - ScreenHeight) / 2` with the component's own size cancelled out, which is the form this port draws about. RECORDED, not ported: `SetRetroAspectViewport` (:98-146), the pillarboxed 4:3 / 16:10 rect that reads the same hudHeight term - it is RetroModeAspectCorrection, a render-to-texture presenter this port has no equivalent of, and the docked-bar term inside it is the one this file now carries. D10's withdrawal held under the ship: the activation ray is the camera's forward vector, and not one activation site changed. 20 mutations, 20 killed at `test/hudlarge.test.js`. WHAT IS LEFT OF THIS ROW: the TextBox single-line edit model. NARROWED TWICE BEFORE THAT: U37 shipped the ToolTip whole AND the MouseEnter/Move model it needed (ui/toolTip.js + overlayHover in both channels and all four hosts), so the inventory's hover info panel now waits only on its own hover(vx,vy); U38 shipped HUDCrosshair and HUDInteractionModeIcon, both riding drawHud - the ONE host-agnostic call - with DFU's placement, the cursor-active suppression and the xhair-replaces-the-crosshair branch verbatim, and their two settings live. What is LEFT of this row: HUDLarge itself, HUDActiveSpells, HUDEscortingNPCFaces (quest-gated), and the TextBox single-line edit model. THE ART DEPARTURE stands for the two that shipped (Ledger A): DFU loads its own authored PNGs from Unity Resources for the crosshair and the four icon sets, which are not ARENA2 data and not in the sparse clone, so the port draws a cross and the mode's NAME in DFU's own slots | Game/UserInterface/HUDLarge.cs, HUDActiveSpells.cs, TextBox.cs, BaseScreenComponent.cs | Audit-25 S-H |

| WorldDataReplacement / BuildingReplacement mod hooks. **NARROWED at M-EXT (2026-08-25): the SOUND half of asset injection ships.** `Utility/AssetInjection/SoundReplacement.cs`'s song path is ported - `systems/musicReplacement.js` answers DFU's TryImportSong shape (name in, bytes or nothing out) behind DFU's own `Settings.AssetInjection` gate, and `MusicService.playSong` asks it before it reaches MIDI.BSA, falling back to the built-in song on a miss, a load failure or a decode failure exactly as TryImportSong answering false does. So `Enhancements/AssetInjection` is no longer 'no mod system (Not planned)' - it has a consumer, the UNAVAILABLE entry is struck and the settings screen no longer says 'Nothing to add yet'. TWO DEPARTURES: DFU seeks `.ogg` alone, a Unity WWW-loader constraint rather than a law about the format, so the port takes a SET with a fixed preference order; and there is no StreamingAssets in a browser, so the folder is a pick stored in IndexedDB beside the ARENA2 ingest - its OWN store, because KEEP (the download diet) rejects every audio extension by design. NOTHING SHIPS: the repo carries no audio and the deploy serves none; the player points at their own files. STILL OUT: the texture, model and world-data halves of AssetInjection, and the mod system proper | AssetInjection | Not planned (mod system) except the SOUND half, shipped at M-EXT |

| **AUDIT 26 (2026-08-26) ADDED THE ROWS BELOW.** The full-codebase parity/bug audit read `src/` against DFU at commit 81e89e9 and confirmed 218 findings under adversarial verification. The 67 bug-severity ones are FIXED and pinned (`git log`: "AUDIT 26 fixes wave 1/2/3", closed green 2026-08-26); the 151 that remain are the rows below - 89 PARITY, where the port and DFU differ somewhere a player can reach, and 62 marked **NIT**, which are technically divergent with negligible impact and are NOT a work queue: a NIT row exists so nobody re-finds it and so a slice touching that seam knows the exact shape of the difference. Every row carries its finding ids so the register can be cross-referenced, and the per-finding evidence - both sides, with the C# and the port site read line by line - lives in `.audit26/remainder.json`. Findings are GROUPED where they are one law seen from two sides; the ids in a row's tag are that row's whole membership. THE LINE NUMBERS ARE THE AUDIT'S OWN READ and the tree has moved under some of them already - the exterior static-NPC slice (commit 0fc1d9e) shifted `worldModes.js`, `world.js` and `exterior.js` the same afternoon - so the MEMBER named in each row is the durable half of the citation and the line is the pointer that found it. Five ids are not rows below because the claim already had a home on this page: F088 struck the rain-loop row above, F108 is the Ledger A biography row, F114 narrowed the Ledger A knightly-promotion row, F187 confirms the residue on the save-load-11 row, F221 confirms the interior clause on the MULTI-SLOT SAVE row | see `.audit26/remainder.json` | routed below |
| ~~**INTERIOR WINDOWS KEEP THE EXTERIOR GLOW** (F001, F002)~~ FIXED (AUDIT 26 render batch 2026-08-27: WINDOW_STYLES gains the `disabled` entry - Color.black OUTRIGHT (MaterialReader.cs:933-935), not a colour times an intensity - and EVERY non-exterior host now writes its own style, which is what the tint being one renderer global demands. All three interior mesh paths are laid out Disabled in DFU (DaggerfallInterior.cs :473 individual models, :517 the combined batch, :1270 action doors) and nothing re-lights them mid-visit, since PlayerEnterExit.cs:713 creates the interior as a ROOT GameObject that DaggerfallLocation's Day/Night re-apply never reaches. The missing entry was worse than absent: `windowEmissionRGB('disabled')` fell through the `|| WINDOW_STYLES.day` guard and answered DAY. WIDER THAN THE ROW, and deliberately: DUNGEONS had the same defect and fixing only interiors would have REGRESSED them, since a dungeon inherited whatever the last host wrote and would have started inheriting `disabled`. SetDungeonTextures (DaggerfallMesh.cs :153-169) calls GetMaterial with no window style, and a window material is BORN DayWindowColor * DayWindowIntensity (MaterialReader.cs:456-461), so both dungeon hosts write `day` explicitly - which also fixes a dungeon entered at night having glowed amber. 7-mutation checked). Original finding:  DaggerfallInterior lays out every interior model with `SetClimate(..., WindowStyle.Disabled)` (:473, :517, :1270), and Disabled means EmissionColor BLACK (MaterialReader.cs:934-936). The port's window emission is a single renderer global (`renderer.js:1114-1115`, bound at :1578) that is only ever day/night/fog: `main.js:67` boots it to `day` before routing, only exterior frames write it (`world.js:3295`, `exterior.js:1622`), the interior block at `worldModes.js:2779-2796` never touches it, and `WINDOW_STYLES` (`windowEmission.js:8-13`) has no disabled entry to write. So inside every shop, tavern and house the glass glows pale blue by day and amber at night where DFU and classic show unlit panes - and the port contradicts its own bible (the Rendering-Arc R2 note) and its own comment one import away (`interior.js:28-30`, the `?interior` dev scene - the NIT half, F002, since only the dev route draws it) | DaggerfallInterior.SetClimate + MaterialReader.SetWindowStyle | Rendering arc |

The port's swim path reads only `input.up`/`input.down` (`motor.js:530-531`) and nothing in `src/player` or `src/scenes` feeds carried weight to the motor at all. SLOW FALLING DESCENDS AT 1.75 m/s WHERE DFU FALLS AT 2.1~~ FIXED (AUDIT 26 movement batch 2026-08-27: SLOWFALL_VELOCITY = 105 * UNITY_FIXED_DT (0.02) = a flat 2.1 m/s, written straight to velY - the constant is coupled to UNITY's fixed step, since ApplyGravity runs inside FixedUpdate (:187, PlayerMotor.cs:275/:357) on a value spent as a velocity (`Move(moveDirection * dt)`, PlayerGroundMotor.cs:86). Original finding:  AcrobatMotor.ApplyGravity writes `moveDirection.y = -slowFallSpeed * Time.deltaTime` inside FixedUpdate (:187), so the constant is coupled to Unity's fixed step - pinned at 0.02 in ProjectSettings/TimeManager.asset - giving 105 * 0.02 = 2.1 m/s. The 60 Hz step is a documented port choice and slowfall is the one law whose magnitude moves with it: buffed descents take ~20% longer. Fall damage is unaffected - slowfall re-anchors the fall start each tick on both sides | AcrobatMotor.ApplyGravity (:187) | Player arc (nit) |

| ~~NIT (F213, F214, F054)~~ **FIXED (NT1 2026-08-28)** (all three: destroy() latches `_ctxDead` FIRST and spawnCorpse's warm window reads it; the pile frees mark `dead` per droppedLoot's own removal protocol; hostMagic grew `destroy()` - retireMissile per flight, candle.clear(), the batches sweep - and dungeonContext.destroy calls it; the no-landing throw frees the built context before propagating. 9-mutation checked at test/resourcesafety.test.js (with F215)) Original finding: THREE TEARDOWN PATHS DROP ALLOCATIONS THE 17e OWNERSHIP RULE ASSIGNS THEM. (1) `dungeonContext.destroy` frees batches and clears `_piles` WITHOUT setting `dead` first (:3066-3069), which is the documented removal protocol (`droppedLoot.js:45-53`), so a pile dropped just before exit whose texture is still warming mints a batch onto an orphan; `spawnCorpse` has the same shape (:1449-1473, guarding only `!f.dead`). (2) `tryEnter` builds the whole interior context - GPU billboard batches plus voxelfolk meshes - before computing the landing and throws "no interior landing" before `interiorCtx` is assigned (`worldModes.js:2224`, :2238), and both hosts only log it, so the build leaks on every E-press at such a door. (3) `destroy()` never reaches the player magic engine's own missiles/batches lists (`hostMagic.js:78-93`, minted at `dungeonContext.js:1117`), which expose no destroy surface, so exiting a dungeon with a spell in flight leaks that missile's VAO and buffers. All three are narrow windows with no symptom short of long-session GPU growth; the rule they break is the port's own | (port-internal: DFU relies on Unity scene teardown) | Systems arc (nit) |
| ~~NIT (F215)~~ **FIXED (NT1 2026-08-28)** (the flag IS the promise - `ensure` returns `this._booted ??= this._boot(fetchBytes)`, music.js's own AUDIT 19 law, with the context-creation sync prefix preserved inside _boot; a concurrent second caller now awaits the whole archive load. Behaviorally pinned: two callers one promise, and the second caller provably unresolved until init finishes) Original finding: `AudioEngine.ensure` GUARDS ITS AWAIT WITH A BOOLEAN. `audio.js:69-88` sets `_booted = true` and returns before `await this.init(fetchBytes)` completes, so a concurrent second caller's await resolves while `enabled` is still false and its immediate one-shots drop. The sibling MusicService diagnosed and fixed exactly this at AUDIT 19 ("a guard set before its own async work is not idempotence, it is a race with a flag on it", `music.js:39-54`) and the sound engine kept the boolean. Per-frame callers self-heal, so the cost is one-shots lost inside the boot window | (port-internal: DFU's audio source is a Unity singleton) | Audio arc (nit) |

AddQuestItem (GameObjectHelper.cs:1128-1141) NEVER rays: its dungeon shift is the CONSTANT -randomTreasureMarkerDim/2 * GlobalScale = -0.5, nothing to do with the sprite's height, so an item on a table, cage or ledge STAYS UP where the port's ray snapped it to the floor. Only AddQuestNPC aligns (:1040), and F069 moved its ray ORIGIN to the billboard's CENTRE + 0.2 (:339) from the base + 0.2 - half a sprite lower - so a tall dungeon NPC no longer starts its ray below a surface DFU clears. Only AddQuestNPC rays (:1040). | ~~**THE ARMOUR MODIFIER CHANNEL IS ADDITIVE WHERE DFU'S SETTERS ARE NON-STACKING MIN-SETS** (F122, F123)~~ FIXED (AUDIT 26 magic batch 2026-08-27: the one `+=` channel is now DFU's TWO min-set channels. Both setters are written as DaggerfallEntity.cs:400-416 writes them - `if (amount < standing) standing = amount` - and both are zeroed at the top of every fold, as :841-842 zeroes them. So repeats within a channel floor at -5 while the two channels stay independent, and a Strengthens item beside a BadReactionsFrom source still reaches -10 between them. F123's inert WeakensArmor is preserved bug-for-bug: `SetDecreasedArmorValueModifier(5)` cannot pass a `< 0` test from a zeroed channel, so the -700 drawback still costs the player nothing. Two consumers, not one: FormulaHelper.cs:1158 ADDS both channels into the struck part's armour value (enchantArmorMod), and RefreshArmourValues (PaperDoll.cs:161) SUBTRACTS them the other way for the paperdoll's labels (enchantArmorDisplayMod) - which retires nativeInventory's own FLAGGED note, since armorLabelValue had carried the parameter for that number since AUDIT 17e F36 with nothing to feed it. 11-mutation checked). Original finding:  DaggerfallEntity's SetIncreasedArmorValueModifier and SetDecreasedArmorValueModifier assign only when the new value beats the standing one and the modifiers are cleared every constant-effects pass (:400-416, :842), which has two consequences the port does not reproduce. (a) Any number of StrengthensArmor items yields exactly -5 in DFU and multiple BadReactionsFrom items likewise floor at -5 (their ChanceToHit term IS additive, :418-421); the port folds each row into `mods.armorMod` with `+=` (`enchantments.js:428`, :527), so two Strengthens items give -10 and the player is measurably harder to hit than DFU allows. (b) WeakensArmor calls `SetDecreasedArmorValueModifier(5)` and `5 < 0` is never true, so the enchantment is INERT in DFU - the port adds +5 into the same channel each round and `combat/formulas.js:292` hands it to the attacker's hit chance, so the drawback the player took for -700 enchantment budget costs defence only here | DaggerfallEntity armour modifier setters (:400-416) | Systems arc |

| ~~**THE EXTERIOR AMBIENCE LOOP FOLLOWS THE PLAYER INDOORS - AND THAT IS NOT DFU** (F088)~~ **REFUTED 2026-08-27 (AUDIT 26 audio batch) - the carry-over IS DFU and stands.** The row's case rests on a SCENE-HIERARCHY claim (that AmbientEffectsPlayer hangs off PlayerEnterExit.ExteriorParent, so DisableAllParents silences it). That claim cannot be read from DFU's source at all - the .unity scenes are not in the tree - and the CODE refutes it: AmbientEffectsPlayer.Update's water arm (:165-175) acts only when `playerEnterExit.blockWaterLevel != 10000`, and blockWaterLevel is assigned in exactly one place, inside `if (dungeon && isPlayerInsideDungeon)` (PlayerEnterExit.cs:328-343). Were the component deactivated on entering a dungeon, that whole arm - its DFRandom rolls, its surface positioning, its classic cadence - could never run; the same goes for the cemetery guard's `!IsPlayerInside` (:154). Update's only early return is IsMuted (:109-110). So nothing stops the loop and the port is right to carry it. The row's attack on the ORIGINAL comment's weak argument was fair - the cemetery guard proved little - so ambientEffects.js now carries the decisive water-arm evidence instead, and this is the SECOND challenge to the same decision, recorded so there is not a third. Original finding:  This CORRECTS the struck rain-loop row above, which read the WeatherManager half correctly and stopped one component short. WeatherAmbientEffects is a child of the scene's Exterior object (PlayerEnterExit.ExteriorParent), and TransitionInterior/TransitionDungeonInterior call DisableAllParents, which does `ExteriorParent.SetActive(false)` (:797, :931, :999, :1037-1050), stopping its AudioSources - OnDisable then clears the loop handles (:96-100). So DFU goes SILENT indoors even though its Presets field still says Rain. The port never stops the loop: `world.js:3068-3086` and `exterior.js:1435-1454` return on modal frames before the ambience update (`world.js:3280`, `exterior.js:1603`), and `ambientEffects.js:86-110` defends the carry-over as bug-for-bug fidelity. Enter a tavern or a dungeon in the rain, or at night with the crickets up, and the outdoor loop plays for the whole visit | PlayerEnterExit.DisableAllParents (:1037-1050) | Audio arc |

| ~~**THE SPELL MAKER IGNORES THE CHOSEN EFFECTS' ALLOWED TARGETS AND ELEMENTS** (F181)~~ FIXED (AUDIT 26 spell-maker batch 2026-08-27: UpdateAllowedButtons ported whole, by the two OPPOSITE rules this row's own text ran together and was corrected in place for earlier today. Targets INTERSECT from TargetFlags_All (:585), so one caster-only effect narrows the whole spell; ELEMENTS UNION from ElementFlags_MagicOnly (:588), so magic is always offered and each effect only adds. The window holds both sets, recomputes on every slot change as DFU does at :226/:338/:396/:462/:1005, and EnforceSelectedButtons moves an outlawed selection to the FIRST legal value in declaration order. The default arm FORCES CasterOnly + Magic rather than merely enforcing (:564-570), so deleting the last effect snaps an Area At Range selection back even though the default set still allows it. Where DFU has five buttons whose SetSpell* returns early on an illegal bit, the port has one cycling row, so the cycle STEPS OVER a disallowed value - the player can no more land on one here than select one there. The per-effect target flags are a new table read off every effect class; the ELEMENT half needed no table at all, since MAGIC_ONLY_KEYS already is that set for the GetElementType law - the two derivations were cross-checked key for key, 72 of 72, and agree exactly. 18-mutation checked, all caught first pass. spellmakerwindow's cycle pin was rewritten: it read the empty sheet's element wrap as freedom, where DFU's default flags leave Magic the only legal element). Original finding:  DFU restricts both: SetSpellTarget returns early when the target bit is not in allowedTargets (:499-501), SetSpellElement likewise (:527-530), and UpdateAllowedButtons forces the selection back to the first legal value (:563-601). CORRECTED 2026-08-27 while the rest of this cluster was fixed: the two flag sets are combined by OPPOSITE rules, which this row ran together. Targets are the INTERSECTION of each chosen effect's AllowedTargets, seeded TargetFlags_All (:585); ELEMENTS are the UNION, seeded ElementFlags_MagicOnly, so magic is always offered and each effect only ADDS to what is legal (:588). With no effects chosen at all the window falls back to defaultTargetFlags/defaultElementFlags and forces CasterOnly + Magic (:564-570). `_inputMain` cycles rangeType and element freely through all five values (`spellMakerWindow.js:187-190`) and no allowedTargets or allowedElements state exists anywhere in `src/`. The port will price and inscribe spells DFU cannot mint - an "Area at Range" built from a caster-only effect - with the target semantics and costs that go with them | DaggerfallSpellMakerWindow.UpdateAllowedButtons (:499-601) | UI arc |
| ~~**TWO HUD VITALS SETTINGS ARE SURFACED AND CONSUMED BY NOTHING** (F148, F149)~~ FIXED (VB1 2026-08-28: ui/hudVitals.js carries all three members whole - HUDVitals' nine-bar rig, VerticalProgressSmoother's 0.4s lerp with its two entry delays (-0.5s cold, -0.25s re-armed), and VitalsChangeDetector's previous-value snapshot with its max-change reset and pause gate. EnableVitalsIndicators ships TRUE, so the damage trail is now the DEFAULT look: on a loss the main bar drops by LostPercent AT ONCE and the dark loss bar lags shut behind it; on a gain the loss bar jumps by GainPercent (-1 * LostPercent) and the main bar climbs; the bright gain bars never smooth-change. Draw order is Components.Add's - losses behind, gains between, art mains on top. TWO RIGS, ONE DETECTOR, which is DFU's own shape (HUDLarge.cs:66 owns a second HUDVitals): both rigs take every change event, only the enabled one cycles, and a HUD-mode flip unprimes the detector exactly as DaggerfallHUD_OnLargeHUDToggle resets it - the idle rig's trail would otherwise stand stale. The SWAP (F149) exchanges the health/fatigue ART and their loss/gain colours with magicka untouched, applied as a draw-time re-mapping (vitalsSkin) so both HUDs swap together; DFU reads it once at construction, the port per frame - the largeHudEnabled posture, recorded in the module header. The detector's IsGamePaused gate rides the active cursor, its nearest port seam; DFU's other reset triggers (world init, load, court) need no seam because those paths boot a host afresh. Both keys are LIVE-tiered with hudVitals.js as consumer. 18 mutations, 18 killed - including the toggle reset, whose absence is masked by the both-rigs law until the enabled trail has closed, and the large HUD's draw arm, which a source regex alone could not kill). Original finding:  HUDVitals adds six indicator bars when EnableVitalsIndicators is true - smoothed loss trails and gain bars driven by the change detector (:99-115, :276-312) - and takes SynchronizeImmediately only when it is FALSE (:211-214); DFU ships it TRUE, so the port's three plain bars (`hud.js:300-310`) are the setting-false path and the damage/heal trail never appears. LoadAssets also swaps the health and fatigue bar art (MAIN03I0/MAIN04I0) and their indicator colours when SwapHealthAndFatigueColors is set (:181-198); `loadHud` maps them unconditionally (`hud.js:188-190`) and `hudLarge.js:349-352` reuses the same unswapped art. Both keys are surfaced on the settings screen and read by no file in `src/`, so two toggles a player can turn on do nothing - and neither was recorded anywhere | HUDVitals (:99-115, :181-198, :211-214) | UI arc |

## D. THE BOARD (next up, in order)

Logged 2026-08-25, at the end of the X11c/T2/T3/P1/H3 run. Section C is
the standing GAP list and answers "what is not ported"; this is the
much shorter WORKING list and answers "what should be picked up next".
Every entry below was re-verified against the tree the day it was
written, in the L1 discipline - a stale board sends the next slice to
build what already ships.

**The rule that keeps this honest: close a row here by striking it and
naming the slice, the same way section C rows are closed. If an entry
is still open after a slice that touched its area, say why.**

### 1. ~~The bank purchase window's 3D model preview~~ SHIPPED (H4 2026-08-27)

The 104x91 panel shows the selected building's own ARCH3D model live,
rotating -1 degree per 0.02s (the window's clock, real-time
accumulated). The split: the WINDOW owns the rotation and the camera
law (houses from (0,3,-20), near 0.7 far 100, Unity's default
60-degree lens; the ships arm's (0,12,SHIP_CAMERA_DIST) pairs sit in
banking.js beside the model ids for the day a ships list exists) and
worldModes owns drawBankModelPreview - the automap's second-beginFrame
precedent cut down to a panel: scissor BEFORE beginFrame so the clear
touches only the display rect, viewport AFTER it (beginFrame sets the
full one), the ONE mirror on the projection, the mesh through the
pipeline's own async getGpuMesh, clear color borrowed and returned.
RECORDED: DFU climate-swaps the preview's textures and lights it with
a 0.4 directional + hard shadows; the port draws base textures under
the collapsed-ambient idiom the automap records. Pins in
`test/bankpreview.test.js`.

### 2. ~~The sixteen `.FLC` summoning videos~~ SHIPPED (G7b 2026-08-27)

`ui/daedraSummonedWindow.js` is DaggerfallDaedraSummonedWindow: the
prince's own film fullscreen and LOOPING (FLCPlayer.Loop's default)
over the F1/F2a reader the constellations built, the quest offer read
over it in FOUR-LINE chunks (a click or any key turns the page), the
last chunk answering to Yes/No through the offer flow's REAL respond -
so accept runs startQuestImmediate and refuse runs the rumor/topic
sweeps - and the answer's own message reading through the same chunks
before the last click closes. ~~The refusal's 3-5 daedra ride a
spawnRefusalFoes door FLAGGED unmounted~~ (the IF slice mounted the
door with its interior pool; CV1 2026-08-27 verified both punishment
arms' numbers against the C# and closed the F194 row with them). The host mounts ONE consumer
of the offer step: the film window when the FLC loads, the box chain
when it cannot - never traps.

~~The standalone `?dungeon` host mounts no trade window, so Identify

`onIdentify`/`onDispelMagic` seams optional-chained into SILENCE

~~What PR1 added on top of that was a REASON: DFU has no standalone
dungeon scene, so the trade window there was never owed.~~ **DR1
2026-09-03 retired the reason and shipped the windows.** "Never owed"
was true of where the two windows were BUILT and false as a refusal.
Both are `isChoiceWindow` natives over host-agnostic art, and
`dungeonContext.js` already owned every seam one needs - it has driven
`NativeInventoryWindow`, the automap and the rest window through the
same stack since U26/S9/D3. The four-hosts reading is the one that
holds: a law that lives in a host lives in every host that has the
surface, and this host HAS the surface. `onIdentify` mounts
`openIdentifySpellWindow` (Identify.cs:71-76 over this host's
bindings, DoModeAction's spell arm :954-995 as its commit) and
`onDispelMagic` mounts the bundle picker
(`dispellableBundles(liveBundles(...))` -> `ListPickerWindow` ->
`dispelBundle`), both through `mountSpellWindow`, which here is this
file's `pushDungeonWindow`. The art warms at BOOT beside PICK00I0,
because X11c's bug - a caster who has never been indoors reading
`tradeArtLoaded()` false - is the same bug one host over. Section C's
`:579` row is struck with it; three DR1 pins in x11b.test.js go red
when either mount is reverted. The refusal line survives as the ART
guard alone, which every host carries.

**RESTOCKED 2026-08-27, at the end of the SAV1-SAV4 run** (the classic

### 5. ~~Save screenshots - the capture half~~ SHIPPED (SS1 2026-08-27)

The save ARMS a request (`requestScreenshot`, on `saveSlot` success in
both composers) and the host frame loop DELIVERS it after its last
draw (`capturePendingScreenshot` in both tails of world.js's and
dungeon.js's loops - preserveDrawingBuffer false makes the buffer
same-task-only), with a two-frame countdown standing in for SaveGame's
two WaitForEndOfFrame yields so the save window has popped, HUD in
shot as the C# leaves it. The WINDOW loads the shot itself
(GetSaveScreenshot -> the panel texture, `_shotTexture`'s (key, url)
cache released on change); `hooks.screenshotTexture` stays the
override seam. RECORDED departure: DFU stores the full-resolution
JPEG on disk, the port downscales to 320x200 for the localStorage
quota. 13 pins mutation-run and killed
(`test/savescreenshot.test.js`).

### 6. ~~The interior save context (AUDIT 26 F221, the multi-slot row's last clause)~~ SHIPPED (IS1 2026-08-27)

The envelope's `interior` slot (additive field) carries the entered
exterior door's identity + the building discovery record with its
insideOpenShop latch; the load re-enters through the ONE transition
core (tryEnter's tail extracted as `enterInteriorCore`) with the
saved position landing raw, the no-door reposition arm verbatim, and
the interior `savingPrevented` stopgap deleted - DFU's one standing
save block is mid-rappel and a building never was one. The section C
row above closes WHOLE with it. 14 pins mutation-run and killed
(`test/interiorsave.test.js`).

### 7. The enhanced menu's full slot pane (PX lane)

`ui/enhancedMenu.js` reads the store for Continue/Delete but its saves
pane shows the most recent slot only - the "More saves" note at
`:332` says so out loud. The pane wants the per-character list the
store already enumerates. PX-lane work (the modern surface), not a
DFU-parity row.

Mac's answer, verbatim: "URL flags are fine, no need." The 36 flags
stay ungated in the production build; the audit row above records the
decision. Closed by a decision, not by work - there was never work to
do.

### RESTOCKED 2026-08-29 (LR1) - AND THE HONEST HEADLINE IS THAT SECTION C IS SPENT

Seven slices shipped since the last restock and none had been recorded
here: **RP1** (townTalk's region read live), **FS1** (the stale-flag
sweep + the "FLAGGED in <file>" guard), **EC1** (the enchant ctx's live
foe pool), **SD1** (the behaviour-free dungeon spawn door + DFU's
missing lineOfSightCheck arm), **IN1** (the open-flags harvest stops
counting quotations and identifiers), **RE1** (the random encounter
uses DFU's placement law) and **DE1** (Mac's report: entering a dungeon
put the player across it - two DFU members, one port function).

**Rows 1-8 above are all closed, decided, reserved or data-gated**, and
the re-sweep that restocked this board found the same of section C:

- **AUDIT 26's tail: 117 rows, 0 unstruck.** Entirely closed.
- **AUDIT 25's tail: 23 rows, 6 unstruck** - and of those six, one is
  Not-planned (TangentSolver), one is Not-planned-but-for-the-shipped-
  half (WorldDataReplacement), two were closed elsewhere and never
  struck here (RegionPowerAndConditions by RS1, the standalone dungeon
  trade window by PR1, which found it was never owed), one is the three
  ARENA2-gated probes, and one is the vampirism row - whose move-sound
  clause LM1 has just taken.
- The four rows the preamble calls the most-read: three closed or
  reserved, as the LR1 note up there records.
- The F5 armour row: closed, and its remaining half described work DFU
  does not have.

**So the routed gap list is not where the next slice comes from.** What
is left in it is reserved for Mac's enhanced lane, waiting on ARENA2
beside the tree, or not planned. That is not a complaint about the
ledger - it is what a ledger looks like when it has been worked
through, and saying so is more useful than restocking it with rows that
are none of open, takeable, or real.

**Where the work has actually been coming from, in order of yield:**

1. **Mac playing the game.** DE1 was one sentence from him and it was a
   real, shipped, player-facing bug that no audit had found - because
   the two DFU members it confounds both look correct in isolation.
2. **Pulling the thread on a slice just shipped.** FS1 -> EC1 -> SD1 ->
   RE1 was one chain: each slice's flag or seam named the next, and
   three of the four were parity bugs rather than missing features.
3. **A fresh audit.** AUDIT 26 found 218 findings and every one is now
   closed; AUDIT 27 was a state-of-the-tree pass. An AUDIT 28 against
   commit 81e89e9 is the obvious next systematic source, and it is the
   one thing on this list that a session can start on its own.
